        ↓
Verify fresh main remains conformant
        ↓
Mark workflow complete and close Issue

The GitHub Issue remains the operational dashboard, work queue, and append-only journal. Git, TaskGraph, Unity manifests, TaskDelivery, and committed evidence remain the technical authorities.

The second human checkpoint

Previously, the workflow stopped after your runtime Unity validation. It now has a second human boundary for the truth-bearing delivery claims.

The downstream agent:

Runs the required authoritative Edit Mode and/or Play Mode tests.
Preserves the exact validation manifests, XML files, and Unity logs.
Creates the TaskDelivery review draft.
Proposes:
Which committed files are actual conformance surfaces.
The semantic role of each selected file.
Which specific evidence artifacts prove each completion gate.
A gate-specific explanation of why that evidence proves the gate.
Hashes that complete proposal.
Writes the proposal and its SHA-256 identity into the Issue.
Changes the Issue to human-owned delivery review.

The agent cannot approve its own proposal. This preserves the existing TaskDelivery rule that selecting conformance surfaces and mapping evidence to gates are human truth judgments, not clerical automation.

Your Issue comment will look like:

## Human delivery evidence review

Decision: APPROVE
Proposal SHA256: `0123456789abcdef...`

Notes:
The selected surfaces and gate evidence accurately describe the completed task.

Or:

## Human delivery evidence review

Decision: REQUEST_CHANGES
Proposal SHA256: `0123456789abcdef...`

Notes:
VAL-002 should use only the Play Mode XML and human runtime validation.
Do not select the scene-builder test as a production conformance surface.

After posting the comment, you add:

nsc-state:agent-ready

The unified GitHub Action then validates:

The Issue is really waiting for delivery review.
The complete event chain is intact.
The decision is exactly APPROVE or REQUEST_CHANGES.
The proposal SHA exactly matches the current proposal.
The proposal was not superseded.
The state label transition is valid.

The results are:

APPROVE
    → agent_ready / merge_closeout

REQUEST_CHANGES
    → agent_ready / delivery_evidence

A requested-change proposal cannot later be reused. The agent must create a new proposal with a new SHA.

Evidence packaging and conformance

After approval, a later generic agent resumes the same Issue and exact branch.

It then:

Materializes the approved TaskDelivery review.
Revalidates every immutable draft field.
Runs TaskDelivery finalization.
Runs record_delivery.py.
Stages only the exact evidence paths authorized by the delivery package.
Runs draft-evidence validation.
Creates the evidence commit.
Pushes without force.
Verifies the remote task branch equals the local evidence commit.
Requires TaskGraph to derive the task as:
conformant

The agent cannot create the pull request while TaskGraph still reports:

not_delivered
needs_testing
rejected

This means the PR is opened only after the task’s committed evidence actually establishes conformance.

Pull request and merge closeout

Once evidence is committed and the PR is open, the agent releases its lease:

agent_ready / merge_closeout

The Issue records the exact evidence commit as the new operational checkout head. It also retains the earlier human-tested implementation commit separately, so those two identities are never confused.

A later generic agent resumes and inspects the pull request.

Checks pending

The agent records that checks are pending, releases its lease, and stops. Another generic run can check again later.

Checks failed

The Issue becomes durably blocked with the failed checks recorded. It does not leave an invisible agent_working lease.

Checks passed

Before merging, the workflow verifies:

The PR is still open.
It is not a draft.
The PR head exactly equals the recorded evidence commit.
The expected branch is still the PR head branch.
Required checks are successful.
The PR is mergeable.
The integration is still based on the authoritative validated mainline.

The merge preserves branch history; it does not squash or rewrite the evidence lineage.

Mainline advancement protection

A critical guard was added here.

Suppose authoritative Unity validation and evidence were created, but main advanced while the PR was waiting. Merging at that point would introduce an integration that was never tested.

The downstream agent now checks:

current origin/main is an ancestor of the validated evidence commit

If not, it refuses to merge and records a durable blocker explaining that the branch must integrate current main, rerun validation, regenerate evidence, and use a replacement PR.

It will not quietly merge an unvalidated integration.

Fresh-main verification

After GitHub reports the PR merged, the agent does not trust the merge response alone.

It:

Creates or refreshes a separate verification clone.
Fetches current origin/main.
Checks out the exact merged main commit.
Runs full TaskGraph validation.
Runs:
python Pipeline/TaskGraph/taskcontrol.py state NSC-### --json
Requires the task still to be:
conformant

Only then does it:

Append the completed workflow event.
Update the Issue dashboard to complete.
Apply nsc-state:complete.
Close the Issue.

The Issue can be closed without breaking the OpenAI supervisor’s final verification; completion is validated from the deterministic terminal receipt rather than trying to rediscover a closed Issue through the open-Issue queue.

Resume behavior after conformance

A significant resume problem was fixed.

Before this work, task observation consulted GitHub coordination primarily while the task was not_delivered. Once the evidence commit made TaskGraph report conformant, a later agent could have failed to rediscover the still-open merge-closeout Issue.

Managed Issues are now phase-authoritative for operational continuation:

TaskGraph: conformant
Issue: agent_ready / merge_closeout
        ↓
Generic agent resumes merge closeout

This does not let an Issue override repository safety. Every resume still revalidates:

Current task-contract SHA-256.
Valid TaskGraph.
Clean controller checkout.
Active implementation task.
single_agent execution scope.
Concrete decomposition state.
Conformant declared dependencies.
Exact branch and recorded commit.
Exact remote identity.

A stale Issue contract or invalid graph becomes a conflict instead of being resumed.

Failure persistence

Downstream failures now become durable Issue events.

Examples include:

Unity executable unavailable.
Required Unity test failure.
Mutated test checkout.
Stale validation manifest.
Invalid TaskDelivery draft.
Proposal no longer matching the draft.
Evidence packaging failure.
TaskGraph not conformant after evidence.
Push identity mismatch.
PR check failure.
Mainline advancing after validation.
Fresh-main post-merge conformance failure.

The downstream OpenAI agent is required to call the bounded blocker action before returning blocked. It cannot simply exit while leaving the Issue in agent_working.

Generic command behavior

Once PR #59 is merged and your local controller checkout is updated, this remains the normal command:

powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1

It resumes the first fully validated agent_ready Issue and routes automatically:

implementation
    → implementation pipeline

repair
    → implementation/ExecutionCrew repair pipeline

delivery_evidence
    → authoritative validation and delivery proposal pipeline

merge_closeout
    → evidence finalization, PR checks, merge, or post-merge verification

An explicit task remains supported:

powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1 `
  -TaskId NSC-### `
  -ExecutionProvider claude

Optional downstream settings:

-UnityExecutable "C:\Program Files\Unity\Hub\Editor\<VERSION>\Editor\Unity.exe"
-OutputRoot "C:\Users\VincentLiguori\Downloads\NoSafeCircleOutput"

PR checks and merge simulation.

One limitation remains important: these are deterministic infrastructure tests using synthetic Git repositories, provider outputs, Unity results, and PR responses. They did not spend live Claude/Codex tokens or run a licensed Unity editor against a real gameplay task.

**You:**

Does this workflow support decomposition?

* * *

**ChatGPT:**

Not end-to-end

The repository has a decomposition pipeline, but the new durable Game Task Agent workflow does not currently route into it.

The current workflow deliberately accepts only tasks that are already:

kind: implementation
execution_scope: single_agent
decomposition_state: concrete
derived_state: not_delivered
dependencies: conformant

That eligibility check is hard-coded before the GitHub Issue workflow is even consulted. A task marked needs_execution_decomposition, human_integration_required, unknown, or otherwise non-concrete will be rejected as ineligible rather than decomposed.

The Issue state machine also has no decomposition phase. Its phases are only:

implementation
repair
unity_runtime_validation
delivery_evidence
merge_closeout

There is no decomposition, decomposition_review, or graph_application route.

What it does support

It supports the results of decomposition after they have already been reviewed and applied to TaskGraph.

For example:

NSC-020 parent
    ↓ human-reviewed decomposition is applied
NSC-051 concrete child
NSC-052 concrete child
NSC-053 concrete child
    ↓
Game Task Agent can select and execute those children

The workflow reads each child’s decomposition_state, dependencies, resource ownership, and derived TaskGraph state. Once a child is a concrete single_agent implementation task with conformant dependencies, the existing implementation → Unity validation → evidence → merge workflow can handle it.

What is separate today

Pipeline/TaskDecomposition already supports:

Selecting decomposition-relevant parent tasks.
D1B.1 single-provider decomposition.
D1B.2 round-robin author/reviewer decomposition.
Deterministic validation of proposed children.
Dependency rewrites.
Aggregate-parent semantics.
Immutable decomposition_result.json and graph_delta.json.
GitHub decomposition closeout guidance.

But those results have authority:

review_only_not_applied

The decomposition system explicitly does not modify TaskGraph or authorize implementation. Human review and the separate D1C graph-application stage are still required.

What happens right now

Given this task:

{
  "id": "NSC-021",
  "execution_scope": "needs_execution_decomposition",
  "decomposition_state": "concrete"
}

the Game Task Agent will effectively reach:

task is not eligible for implementation coordination

It will not automatically do this:

select NSC-021
    ↓
run round-robin decomposition
    ↓
publish graph-delta review
    ↓
get human approval
    ↓
apply graph delta
    ↓
commit children
    ↓
select first ready child

That routing is missing.

What should be added

To make the workflow genuinely support decomposition, the generic entry point should classify the selected work before applying implementation eligibility:

selected task
    ├─ concrete + single_agent
    │      → existing implementation workflow
    │
    └─ decomposition-relevant
           → decomposition workflow

The decomposition route should:

Use TaskDecomposition.context_builder.validate_task_selection() as the eligibility authority.
Acquire the existing parent task’s durable Issue lease with:
work_type: decomposition
Run D1B.2 round-robin decomposition in its read-only container.
Record exact run, result, graph-delta, source-commit, and hash identities in the Issue.
Stop for human review without applying anything.
After approval, run deterministic D1C graph application in a task branch.
Commit and push the task-contract, dependency, resource-group, and ID-map changes.
Validate the complete proposed TaskGraph.
Merge the graph change through its own PR.
Allow later generic agents to select the newly created concrete children.

So the accurate status is:

Repository decomposition capability: YES
Execution of already-decomposed child tasks: YES
Automatic decomposition inside this durable workflow: NO
Automatic human-reviewed graph application: NO
Automatic continuation from parent to child: NO

That is a real gap. The workflow is complete for concrete implementation tasks, but it is not yet a complete generic task orchestrator until decomposition is routed through the same Issue-based resume system.

**You:**

Do you think we should test a task first before we try to add decomposition into this workflow?

* * *

**ChatGPT:**

Yes. We should test one real, already-concrete task before adding decomposition.

Adding decomposition now would introduce another large set of states—parent selection, multi-model proposal, review, graph-delta approval, graph application, new child tasks, dependency rewrites, and parent-to-child continuation. If the first live run then fails, we would not know whether the problem came from the implementation workflow or the decomposition layer.

Right now, the highest-value action is to prove this path with real infrastructure:

Concrete task
→ Issue lease
→ checkout and branch
→ ExecutionCrew
→ commit and push
→ your Unity checklist
→ your PASS
→ authoritative Unity tests
→ delivery-evidence proposal
→ your approval
→ evidence commit
→ TaskGraph conformant
→ PR
→ merge
→ post-merge conformance
→ Issue complete

The deterministic tests are useful, but they have not yet proven the messy real-world boundaries:

OpenAI API behavior and tool sequencing
Claude/Codex authentication inside Docker
Windows path handling
Real repository path selection
Real ExecutionCrew output
Unity licensing and batch-mode behavior
GitHub Issue permissions and label-triggered Actions
Real evidence packaging
PR checks and merge behavior
Resumption after closing the terminal or starting a different agent

Those are exactly the errors you originally wanted to stop manually shepherding.

The right first test

Use a task that is:

active
kind: implementation
execution_scope: single_agent
decomposition_state: concrete
not_delivered
dependent only on currently conformant tasks
small and locally testable
limited to a narrow script-and-test surface
manually verifiable in Unity in roughly five to ten minutes
not dependent on new art, audio, addressables, or broad scene restructuring
not sharing a heavily contested scene or foundational system with other active work

For the first run, I would use an explicit task ID, rather than asking the agent to select one. That removes generic task selection as another source of uncertainty. Once the explicit-task path succeeds, the next run can test generic selection.

How I would stage the live proof
1. Merge the workflow PR

A complete live test should happen after PR #59 is reviewed, green, and merged. Some critical behavior—especially the GitHub Action that interprets your Issue label transition—needs to exist on main. Testing only from the feature branch would not prove the actual repository workflow.

Then update the clean controller checkout:

cd C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\NoSafeCircle
git switch main
git pull --ff-only origin main
python Pipeline/TaskGraph/taskcontrol.py validate
2. Start one explicit concrete task
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1 `
  -TaskId NSC-### `
  -ExecutionProvider claude

That should work until the Issue becomes:

human_action_required / unity_runtime_validation

At that point, the agent’s implementation should already be committed and pushed. You should not need to inspect or apply a patch.

3. Perform the Issue checklist

Use the exact checkout, branch, and commit recorded in the Issue. Post PASS or FAIL and add:

nsc-state:agent-ready

Then close the terminal. This is important: the next phase should be resumed by a fresh generic invocation, proving that the Issue—not chat history or an in-memory process—is the workflow authority.

4. Resume generically
powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File .\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1

The agent should find the existing Issue and enter delivery_evidence, without selecting another task.

5. Approve the delivery proposal

Review the proposal shown in the Issue, post the hash-bound approval comment, and apply nsc-state:agent-ready again.

Then run the same generic command. It should package evidence, establish TaskGraph conformance, open the PR, and durably pause if checks are still running.

6. Complete merge closeout

Run the same generic command after checks finish. It should merge only the exact validated head, verify conformance from a fresh main clone, complete the Issue, and close it.

What counts as success

We should not call the live test successful merely because ExecutionCrew writes code. The canary succeeds only when all of these are true:

The task’s Issue contains a complete, understandable history.
A fresh agent can resume after every human boundary.
The task branch is committed and pushed before your Unity work.
Your PASS or FAIL is bound to the exact tested commit.
Authoritative Unity artifacts identify that same commit and tree.
Delivery evidence is human-approved rather than self-approved by the agent.
TaskGraph derives conformant from committed evidence.
The PR merges without rewriting history.
A fresh main clone still derives the task as conformant.
The Issue ends in complete and is closed.
At no point do you need to reconstruct a command, run ID, output directory, or prior chat context manually.
When to add decomposition

After one full happy-path task succeeds, fix whatever the live run exposes. Then I would run one more task—or exercise a genuine FAIL/repair cycle—to prove resumption and repair are dependable.

Only then add:

decomposition-relevant parent
→ round-robin decomposition
→ human graph review
→ deterministic graph application
→ graph-change PR
→ concrete child selection
→ existing task workflow

That order keeps debugging tractable.

The next milestone should be a completed real task, not another infrastructure feature.

**ChatGPT:**

We are ready to run the first real task

While preparing the live run, I found and fixed three stale test/workflow references. The exact current PR head, ddcbf27f00d8b716403fb91dacf4f8862a7e0980, now passes the full Windows deterministic suite. PR #59 is no longer a draft and is currently open and mergeable.

We will use NSC-020 — Shared Doorway-Crossing State. It is active, concrete, single-agent work with no dependencies, so it removes dependency readiness from the first live experiment. It also has no existing managed Issue, so the run will test Issue creation from scratch.

1. Paste this entire block into PowerShell

This will:

Verify your controller checkout is clean.
Verify PR #59 is still the exact green head I tested.
Merge it with history preserved.
Update local main.
Validate TaskGraph.
Install the OpenAI Agents SDK requirement.
Verify GitHub, Docker, and the shared Claude authentication volume.
Read your OpenAI API key securely when necessary.
Perform a read-only NSC-020 preflight.
Start the real goal-oriented agent.
$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

$Repository = "C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\NoSafeCircle"
$WorkflowPullRequest = 59
$ExpectedWorkflowHead = "ddcbf27f00d8b716403fb91dacf4f8862a7e0980"
$TaskId = "NSC-020"
$ExecutionProvider = "claude"
$OutputRoot = Join-Path $env:USERPROFILE "Downloads\NoSafeCircleOutput"

Set-Location $Repository

Write-Host "`n=== 1. Verify clean controller checkout ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

$DirtyPaths = @(git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all)

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "Unable to inspect the controller checkout."
}

if ($DirtyPaths.Count -gt 0) {
    git status --short --untracked-files=all
    throw "The controller checkout is dirty. Do not merge or start the agent until these files are reconciled."
}

Write-Host "`n=== 2. Verify and merge the exact green workflow PR ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

$PullRequest = gh pr view $WorkflowPullRequest `
    --repo cathode26/NoSafeCircle `
    --json headRefOid,isDraft,state,mergedAt | ConvertFrom-Json

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "Unable to inspect PR #$WorkflowPullRequest."
}

if ($PullRequest.headRefOid -ne $ExpectedWorkflowHead) {
    throw "PR #$WorkflowPullRequest changed after validation. Expected $ExpectedWorkflowHead but found $($PullRequest.headRefOid)."
}

if ($PullRequest.isDraft) {
    throw "PR #$WorkflowPullRequest unexpectedly returned to draft state."
}

if ($null -eq $PullRequest.mergedAt) {
    gh pr checks $WorkflowPullRequest --repo cathode26/NoSafeCircle

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "PR #$WorkflowPullRequest does not currently have passing checks."
    }

    gh pr merge $WorkflowPullRequest `
        --repo cathode26/NoSafeCircle `
        --merge `
        --match-head-commit $ExpectedWorkflowHead

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "PR #$WorkflowPullRequest could not be merged."
    }
}
else {
    Write-Host "PR #$WorkflowPullRequest is already merged."
}

Write-Host "`n=== 3. Update and validate local main ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

git switch main

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "Could not switch the controller checkout to main."
}

git pull --ff-only origin main

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "Could not fast-forward local main."
}

$LocalHead = (git rev-parse HEAD).Trim()
$RemoteHead = (git rev-parse origin/main).Trim()

if ($LocalHead -ne $RemoteHead) {
    throw "Local main does not equal origin/main. Local=$LocalHead Remote=$RemoteHead"
}

python Pipeline/TaskGraph/taskcontrol.py validate

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "TaskGraph validation failed after merging the workflow."
}

if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1" -PathType Leaf)) {
    throw "The merged Game Task Agent launcher is missing."
}

Write-Host "`n=== 4. Prepare the OpenAI supervisor ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

python -m pip install --disable-pip-version-check -r Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/requirements.txt

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "The OpenAI Agents SDK requirements could not be installed."
}

python -c "import agents"

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "The OpenAI Agents SDK is still unavailable after installation."
}

if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($env:OPENAI_API_KEY)) {
    $SecureApiKey = Read-Host "Paste your OPENAI_API_KEY; the value will not be displayed" -AsSecureString
    $ApiCredential = New-Object System.Net.NetworkCredential("", $SecureApiKey)
    $env:OPENAI_API_KEY = $ApiCredential.Password
}

if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($env:OPENAI_API_KEY)) {
    throw "OPENAI_API_KEY was not supplied."
}

Write-Host "`n=== 5. Verify GitHub, Docker, and Claude configuration ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

gh auth status --hostname github.com

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "GitHub CLI authentication is unavailable. Run: gh auth login"
}

docker compose version

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "Docker Desktop or Docker Compose is unavailable."
}

docker volume inspect nosafecircle_claude-config | Out-Null

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "The shared Claude authentication volume nosafecircle_claude-config is missing."
}

docker compose -p nosafecircle build claude-exec

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "The claude-exec image could not be built."
}

Write-Host "`n=== 6. Perform read-only NSC-020 preflight ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

$MachineName = ($env:COMPUTERNAME.ToLowerInvariant() -replace "[^a-z0-9]+", "-").Trim("-")
$WorkerId = "task-review-agent-$MachineName-$([Guid]::NewGuid().ToString("N").Substring(0, 10))"

$ObservationText = (& python `
    Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/run_pipeline_agent.py `
    --mode observe `
    --source $Repository `
    --worker-id $WorkerId `
    --execution-provider $ExecutionProvider `
    --task-id $TaskId | Out-String).Trim()

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "The read-only NSC-020 observation failed."
}

$Observation = $ObservationText | ConvertFrom-Json
$ObservedTask = $Observation.observation.task
$ObservedEnvironment = $Observation.observation.environment

if ($ObservedTask.task_id -ne $TaskId) {
    throw "The observer returned the wrong task: $($ObservedTask.task_id)"
}

if ($ObservedTask.contract_disposition -ne "active") {
    throw "$TaskId is not active."
}

if ($ObservedTask.kind -ne "implementation") {
    throw "$TaskId is not an implementation task."
}

if ($ObservedTask.execution_scope -ne "single_agent") {
    throw "$TaskId is not single-agent work."
}

if ($ObservedTask.decomposition_state -ne "concrete") {
    throw "$TaskId is not concrete."
}

if ($ObservedTask.derived_state -ne "not_delivered") {
    throw "$TaskId is not currently not_delivered. Current state: $($ObservedTask.derived_state)"
}

if (-not [bool]$ObservedTask.dependencies_conformant) {
    throw "$TaskId has a nonconformant dependency."
}

if (-not [bool]$ObservedEnvironment.ready) {
    $EnvironmentErrors = @($ObservedEnvironment.errors) -join [Environment]::NewLine
    throw "The production environment is not ready:`n$EnvironmentErrors"
}

Write-Host "Preflight passed:" -ForegroundColor Green
Write-Host "  Task: $TaskId — $($ObservedTask.title)"
Write-Host "  State: $($ObservedTask.derived_state)"
Write-Host "  Dependencies conformant: $($ObservedTask.dependencies_conformant)"
Write-Host "  Worker: $WorkerId"
Write-Host "  Output root: $OutputRoot"

Write-Host "`n=== 7. Start the real NSC-020 workflow ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass `
    -File .\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1 `
    -TaskId $TaskId `
    -ExecutionProvider $ExecutionProvider `
    -WorkerId $WorkerId `
    -OutputRoot $OutputRoot `
    -MaxTurns 120

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "The Game Task Agent stopped with exit code $LASTEXITCODE. Do not manually finish the implementation; inspect the durable Issue and terminal result."
}

The fixed Compose project name is intentional. It allows the task checkout to remain isolated while reusing the authenticated nosafecircle_claude-config volume rather than creating an empty clone-specific volume.

Docker permissions now

The execution container has exactly these relevant filesystem permissions:

Container path	Host path	Permission	Purpose
/workspace	The isolated task checkout, such as C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\NSC-020	Read-only	Source inspected by ExecutionCrew
/execution-output	<task checkout>\Pipeline\ExecutionCrew\outputs	Read/write	Crew logs, crew_result.json, and candidate.patch
/home/agent/.claude	nosafecircle_claude-config volume	Read/write	Claude authentication and configuration

That means Claude’s Docker process cannot directly edit the actual task checkout. It works in disposable internal clones and writes only its output artifacts to /execution-output. The host-side deterministic coordinator verifies the candidate and then applies, tests, commits, and pushes it.

ExecutionCrew’s host output is now explicitly:

C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\NSC-020\Pipeline\ExecutionCrew\outputs

That directory is ignored by Git, so its generated artifacts do not dirty the task branch.

Downloads are no longer the default

The launcher now defaults all longer-lived validation and delivery-review output to:

C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\.task-review-agent\outputs

The overall structure will be approximately:

C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\
├── NoSafeCircle\                         # clean controller checkout
├── NSC-020\                              # isolated task checkout
│   └── Pipeline\ExecutionCrew\outputs\   # ignored Docker crew output
└── .task-review-agent\
    ├── NSC-020.*.json                    # durable local workflow receipts
    └── outputs\
        └── NSC-020\
            └── delivery-<commit>\
                ├── validation\
                ├── delivery-review-draft.json
                ├── delivery-proposal-01.json
                └── delivery-spec.json

Nothing in the normal workflow needs to use your Downloads directory now.

The launcher now performs a real permission test

Before it creates or claims any task Issue, the launcher now:

Confirms the shared provider volume exists.
Starts the selected claude-exec or codex-exec service.
Attempts to write into /workspace and requires that attempt to fail.
Confirms the provider configuration directory is readable and writable.
Writes a unique probe file into /execution-output.
Confirms the probe appears on the Windows host.
Verifies its contents.
Deletes the probe.

If any part fails, it stops before it claims NSC-020 or creates its branch.

That last point matters: GitHub Actions can validate the Compose configuration and launcher syntax, but it cannot prove your own Docker Desktop drive-sharing and Windows bind-mount behavior. The new launcher proves that locally at the start of every real run.

$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

$Repository = "C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\NoSafeCircle"
$WorkflowPullRequest = 59
$ExpectedWorkflowHead = "a54979191eb112d54ad86bd34393ecd7bc7eb20b"
$TaskId = "NSC-020"
$ExecutionProvider = "claude"

Set-Location $Repository

Write-Host "`n=== 1. Verify the controller checkout is clean ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

$DirtyPaths = @(git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all)

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "Unable to inspect the controller checkout."
}

if ($DirtyPaths.Count -gt 0) {
    git status --short --untracked-files=all
    throw "The controller checkout is dirty. Reconcile these files before merging or starting a task."
}

Write-Host "`n=== 2. Verify GitHub authentication ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

gh auth status --hostname github.com

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "GitHub CLI authentication is unavailable. Run: gh auth login"
}

Write-Host "`n=== 3. Verify the exact validated workflow PR ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

$PullRequestText = gh pr view $WorkflowPullRequest `
    --repo cathode26/NoSafeCircle `
    --json number,state,isDraft,headRefOid,mergedAt

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "Unable to inspect PR #$WorkflowPullRequest."
}

$PullRequest = $PullRequestText | ConvertFrom-Json

if ($PullRequest.headRefOid -ne $ExpectedWorkflowHead) {
    throw @"
PR #$WorkflowPullRequest changed after validation.

Expected head:
$ExpectedWorkflowHead

Current head:
$($PullRequest.headRefOid)

Do not merge or run the task until the new head is reviewed and validated.
"@
}

if ($PullRequest.isDraft) {
    throw "PR #$WorkflowPullRequest unexpectedly returned to draft state."
}

if ($null -eq $PullRequest.mergedAt) {
    if ($PullRequest.state -ne "OPEN") {
        throw "PR #$WorkflowPullRequest is neither open nor merged. State: $($PullRequest.state)"
    }

    Write-Host "`nChecking the exact PR head..." -ForegroundColor Cyan

    gh pr checks $WorkflowPullRequest --repo cathode26/NoSafeCircle

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "PR #$WorkflowPullRequest does not currently have passing checks."
    }

    Write-Host "`nMerging PR #$WorkflowPullRequest with history preserved..." -ForegroundColor Cyan

    gh pr merge $WorkflowPullRequest `
        --repo cathode26/NoSafeCircle `
        --merge `
        --match-head-commit $ExpectedWorkflowHead

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "PR #$WorkflowPullRequest could not be merged."
    }

    $MergedPullRequest = gh pr view $WorkflowPullRequest `
        --repo cathode26/NoSafeCircle `
        --json mergedAt,mergeCommit | ConvertFrom-Json

    if ($null -eq $MergedPullRequest.mergedAt) {
        throw "GitHub did not confirm that PR #$WorkflowPullRequest was merged."
    }

    Write-Host "Merged commit: $($MergedPullRequest.mergeCommit.oid)" -ForegroundColor Green
}
else {
    Write-Host "PR #$WorkflowPullRequest is already merged." -ForegroundColor Green
}

Write-Host "`n=== 4. Update the clean controller main checkout ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

git switch main

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "Could not switch the controller checkout to main."
}

git pull --ff-only origin main

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "Could not fast-forward local main."
}

$LocalHead = (git rev-parse HEAD).Trim()
$RemoteHead = (git rev-parse origin/main).Trim()

if ($LocalHead -ne $RemoteHead) {
    throw "Local main does not equal origin/main. Local=$LocalHead Remote=$RemoteHead"
}

$DirtyPaths = @(git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all)

if ($DirtyPaths.Count -gt 0) {
    git status --short --untracked-files=all
    throw "The controller checkout became dirty after updating main."
}

Write-Host "Controller main: $LocalHead" -ForegroundColor Green

Write-Host "`n=== 5. Validate TaskGraph ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

python Pipeline/TaskGraph/taskcontrol.py validate

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "TaskGraph validation failed after merging the workflow."
}

if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1" -PathType Leaf)) {
    throw "The merged Game Task Agent launcher is missing."
}

Write-Host "`n=== 6. Install and verify the OpenAI supervisor runtime ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

python -m pip install `
    --disable-pip-version-check `
    -r Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/requirements.txt

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "The OpenAI Agents SDK requirements could not be installed."
}

python -c "import agents; print('OpenAI Agents SDK: READY')"

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "The OpenAI Agents SDK is unavailable after installation."
}

if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($env:OPENAI_API_KEY)) {
    $SecureApiKey = Read-Host `
        "Paste your OPENAI_API_KEY; the value will not be displayed" `
        -AsSecureString

    $ApiCredential = New-Object `
        System.Net.NetworkCredential("", $SecureApiKey)

    $env:OPENAI_API_KEY = $ApiCredential.Password
}

if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($env:OPENAI_API_KEY)) {

docker compose version

docker volume inspect nosafecircle_claude-config | Out-Null

**ChatGPT:**

Press Ctrl+C at that prompt. You do not need to enter anything until we determine where your existing credential is.

It is asking because the current PowerShell process does not contain an OPENAI_API_KEY environment variable:

[string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($env:OPENAI_API_KEY)

returned True, so the block called Read-Host.

There are two separate authentication systems in this workflow:

Claude ExecutionCrew inside Docker uses the persisted nosafecircle_claude-config Docker volume.
The goal-oriented OpenAI supervisor running in host Python uses the OpenAI Agents SDK. By default, that SDK looks for OPENAI_API_KEY. OpenAI GitHub (https://openai.github.io/openai-agents-python/config/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

Your ChatGPT/Codex sign-in is not automatically exposed to arbitrary host-side Python programs as $env:OPENAI_API_KEY. Codex CLI can store credentials created through “Sign in with ChatGPT,” but those credentials remain separate from the ChatGPT session and from the environment variable our Python launcher expects. OpenAI Help Center (https://help.openai.com/en/articles/11381614-api-codex-cli-and-sign-in-with-chatgpt?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

The launcher currently explicitly requires $env:OPENAI_API_KEY, which is why it stopped here.

Check whether the key is already persisted elsewhere

After pressing Ctrl+C, run this. It reports only whether a value exists; it does not print the key:

$ProcessKeyExists = -not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace(
    [Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable("OPENAI_API_KEY", "Process")
)

$UserKeyExists = -not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace(
    [Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable("OPENAI_API_KEY", "User")
)

$MachineKeyExists = -not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace(
    [Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable("OPENAI_API_KEY", "Machine")
)

[pscustomobject]@{
    Process = $ProcessKeyExists
    User    = $UserKeyExists
    Machine = $MachineKeyExists
}
If User is True

Your key is already saved, but this PowerShell window did not inherit it. Load it into the current process:

$env:OPENAI_API_KEY = [Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable(
    "OPENAI_API_KEY",
    "User"
)

if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($env:OPENAI_API_KEY)) {
    throw "The user-level OPENAI_API_KEY could not be loaded."
}

Write-Host "OPENAI_API_KEY loaded into this PowerShell process." -ForegroundColor Green
If Machine is True

Use:

$env:OPENAI_API_KEY = [Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable(
    "OPENAI_API_KEY",
    "Machine"
)

if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($env:OPENAI_API_KEY)) {
    throw "The machine-level OPENAI_API_KEY could not be loaded."
}

Write-Host "OPENAI_API_KEY loaded into this PowerShell process." -ForegroundColor Green
If all three are False

Then the key is not stored as an environment variable. What you probably already have is Codex/ChatGPT CLI authentication, not a host-side API key available to the Agents SDK.

That means I made an incorrect assumption when I said the workflow should already have everything it needed. The Claude worker is authenticated, but the OpenAI manager uses a separate credential path.

The clean fix is to change the launcher so it retrieves the supervisor credential from secure Windows storage rather than asking you to paste it during each run. Until that change is made, the immediate alternatives are either supplying an API key to this PowerShell process or replacing the Agents SDK supervisor with a Codex-CLI-authenticated supervisor. The latter is a larger architectural change.

    --repo cathode26/NoSafeCircle `
    --json headRefOid,mergedAt,state | ConvertFrom-Json

if ($FixPr.headRefOid -ne $ExpectedFixHead) {
    throw "PR #$FixPullRequest changed. Expected $ExpectedFixHead but found $($FixPr.headRefOid)."
}

if ($null -eq $FixPr.mergedAt) {
    gh pr merge $FixPullRequest `
        --repo cathode26/NoSafeCircle `
        --merge `
        --match-head-commit $ExpectedFixHead

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "PR #$FixPullRequest could not be merged."
    }
}
else {
    Write-Host "PR #$FixPullRequest is already merged." -ForegroundColor Green
}

git switch main

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "Could not switch to main."
}

git pull --ff-only origin main

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "Could not update local main."
}

$RemoteHead = (git rev-parse origin/main).Trim()

if ($LocalHead -ne $RemoteHead) {
    throw "Local main does not match origin/main."
}

    -LiteralPath ".\compose.override.yaml" `
    -Raw `
    -Encoding UTF8

if ($ComposeOverride -notmatch "(?ms)task-supervisor-codex-config:\s*\r?\n\s*external:\s*true") {
    throw "The external Codex credential-volume fix is missing from local main."
}

python Pipeline/TaskGraph/taskcontrol.py validate

if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    throw "TaskGraph validation failed."
}

**ChatGPT:**

This was a second, separate PowerShell bug

The external-volume fix worked: Docker no longer complained that the credential volume needed external: true.

The new failure happened because Docker printed this completely normal progress message to stderr:

Container nosafecircle-codex-supervisor-run-... Creating

Windows PowerShell 5.1 converted that stderr output into a NativeCommandError. Because the launcher had:

$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

PowerShell terminated the script before it could inspect Docker’s real exit code. The uploaded transcript shows the stop occurring exactly at the codex login status preflight—not during authentication, Issue creation, or task implementation. 

I fixed that by routing the launcher’s native GitHub and Docker commands through a wrapper that:

temporarily treats native stderr as captured text;
preserves the actual process exit code;
restores ErrorActionPreference;
still distinguishes a real nonzero exit from harmless stderr output.

The exact Windows PowerShell 5.1 regression test passed, along with the complete TaskReviewAgent suite, on PR #61 head 133315bf3459af48aacd94b8cc4369e714f4949f.

I merged PR #61. Current remote main is now:

4c777efa2fe9b4aa1a54d320a939350571fadf1f

There is still no NSC-020 Issue, confirming the failed attempt made no operational task changes.

was unrelated. It happened because the if block and else block were submitted as separate interactive PowerShell inputs. The replacement below is enclosed in one & { ... } block, so that cannot happen.

        throw "Could not read local HEAD."
    }

Pasted text(20260828-084249).txt

* * *

**ChatGPT:**

This run got through every preflight and reached the first real Codex supervisor decision. The failure is no longer Docker permissions, credential reuse, GitHub authentication, or line endings. It occurred inside the structured Codex call before the workflow created an NSC-020 Issue or checkout. 

What was wrong

The supervisor sends Codex a JSON Schema through --output-schema. Its nested arguments object declared many unused action parameters as nullable, but did not include them in the object’s required array.

OpenAI Structured Outputs require every declared field to be required, including fields that allow null. Codex supports both gpt-5.6-sol and --output-schema, so the model and command-line feature were valid; our schema was not strict enough. OpenAI Developers+2OpenAI Developers+2 (https://developers.openai.com/codex/cli/reference)

The existing adapter also hid the useful Codex JSONL error event and displayed only:

Codex exited with status 1

I fixed both problems:

Nullable optional action fields are converted into required nullable fields before the schema reaches Codex.
A nonnullable optional field now fails closed instead of having its meaning silently changed.
Codex error and failed JSONL events are now surfaced without printing prompts, credentials, or unrelated transcript contents.
New tests cover recursive schema normalization and bounded provider diagnostics.

The fix is merged into main at:

        throw "Local main does not equal origin/main."

Unexpected main commit.

Expected:
$ExpectedMain

Found:
$LocalHead
"@

Which test confirms each step
Checklist step	Test that proves it	What a green result means

**You:**

I dont think I want it to know to resume it. I think I want it to prefer closing and committing tasks that are ready to be closed and committed.
What do you think about that preference. This way we could use the same generic do work command?

* * *

**ChatGPT:**

Yes. That is the better scheduling policy.

I would call it finish-first scheduling:

A generic “do work” command should finish the most advanced actionable task before starting or resuming earlier-stage work.

The agent should not be invoked with the intent “resume NSC-020.” It should be invoked with the intent:

Find the highest-priority actionable work in the repository and advance it.

The Issue state tells the agent what work exists; you should not have to tell it which Issue to resume.

Why this is better

Finishing existing work gives you several benefits:

Fewer partially completed branches and Issues.
Faster TaskGraph conformance.
Faster dependency unlocking for later tasks.
Less chance that a tested implementation becomes stale while waiting.
A much simpler habit: run one generic command whenever you are ready for the agent to work.
Better recovery after distraction because the repository decides what matters next.

Issue #64 is currently:

agent_ready / delivery_evidence
human_result: pass

That means the implementation is already committed, pushed, and human-tested. It is much closer to being completed than any new implementation task, so it should clearly outrank starting something new.

The current selector is not smart enough yet

Right now, the generic selector gets the validated agent-ready list and chooses:

selected = ready[0]

The ready list is effectively ordered by Issue number, not by closeness to completion.

Your previous queue output even described its policy as:

resume_agent_ready_before_new_task

That is better than ignoring unfinished work, but it is too crude. It means “take an agent-ready Issue,” not “finish the task closest to completion.” 

With several ready Issues, it could select an implementation-phase Issue while another task is one successful merge away from being complete.

The priority should be based on the next action

Phase alone is not quite enough. For example, two Issues can both be in merge_closeout, but:

one may have passing checks and be ready to merge;
another may merely be waiting for checks.

The selector should inspect every valid candidate read-only and rank its deterministic next_action.

I recommend this order:

Priority	Actionable work
1	Verify an already-merged PR on fresh main, mark complete, close Issue
2	Merge an exact evidence commit whose PR checks passed
3	Finalize approved delivery evidence, commit it, establish conformance, open PR
4	Run delivery-evidence work after human Unity PASS
5	Repair an implementation after human FAIL
6	Continue an already-started implementation Issue
7	Select and begin a brand-new task

That is the right order because each row generally represents more completed work than the row beneath it.

Waiting work must not block useful work

The scheduler also needs an actionability check.

It should skip:

human_action_required
blocked
agent_working by another worker
merge_closeout with checks still pending and no useful action available

A PR waiting on checks should not be selected on every generic run while other useful work exists. The agent can inspect it once, record that checks are pending, and apply a next-poll time or simply rank it below currently actionable work.

Otherwise, a waiting closeout Issue could starve the rest of the project.

Selection should be deterministic

Conceptually:

candidates = all_valid_agent_ready_issues()

observed = [
    read_only_observe(candidate)
    for candidate in candidates
]

actionable = [
    candidate
    for candidate in observed
    if candidate.has_useful_next_action
]

selected = sorted(
    actionable,
    key=lambda item: (
        closeout_priority(item.next_action),
        item.ready_since,
        item.issue_number,
    ),
)[0]

The log should then say why it selected the work:

Selected Issue #64
Task: NSC-020
Phase: delivery_evidence
Next action: run_authoritative_unity_test
Selection reason: finish-first priority 4; no higher-priority actionable closeout exists

That makes the scheduler understandable rather than mysterious.

One generic command

After implementing this selection policy, your normal command remains:

Set-Location "C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\NoSafeCircle"

git switch main
git pull --ff-only origin main

powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass `
    -File .\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1 `
    -ExecutionProvider claude `
    -MaxTurns 120

No -TaskId. No “resume Issue #64.” No special closeout command.

The same command would:

close a merge-ready task
        or
finish delivery evidence
        or
repair a human failure
        or
continue implementation
        or

Why the loop occurs

The downstream controller says:

checkout is not ready
→ next action is prepare_task_checkout

It does not distinguish between a missing checkout that can be prepared and a conflicting checkout that cannot safely be repaired automatically.

The checkout manager, however, is intentionally fail-closed. It marks the checkout as conflicting when, among other things:

The checkout working tree is dirty.
Its local origin/main tracking reference is stale.
Its branch or HEAD differs from the Issue.
Its remote task branch differs from the recorded handoff commit.
Its durable manifest conflicts with the current task identity.

When prepare_task_checkout encounters that conflict, it returns:

status: blocked

without changing anything.

The goal loop then makes the key mistake:

The function returned normally
→ log action_completed
→ observe unchanged state
→ ask Codex again
→ Codex selects prepare_task_checkout again

So action_completed currently means only “the Python method returned,” not “the workflow advanced.”

    $ControllerStatus = @(git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all)

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {

    }

        throw "The controller checkout is dirty."

    }

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {

        $env:PYTHONUTF8 = "1"

            --source $Repository

        if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
            throw "The durable Issue queue is invalid or unreadable."

        }

            $env:PYTHONUTF8 = $PreviousPythonUtf8

What the generic agent does now

When it encounters:

Issue: agent_ready / delivery_evidence
Task branch: human-tested
Current main: newer than task branch

it will select:

integrate_current_main

The deterministic host—not Codex—then:

Fetches current origin/main
        ↓
Calculates the task/main merge base
        ↓
Lists every path changed on main
        ↓
Lists every path changed by the task
        ↓
Classifies the drift
        ↓
Creates a normal two-parent merge commit
        ↓
Runs git diff --check
        ↓
Runs TaskGraph validation
        ↓
Pushes the exact merge commit to the task branch
        ↓
Routes conditional revalidation

It never rebases, squashes, force-pushes, or rewrites the commit you tested.

The resulting history is:

                       current main
                           │
                           ▼
human-tested task ───► merge commit
       │                   │
       └── first parent    └── second parent: current main
Automation-only mainline drift

These are the only paths classified as automation-only:

.github/workflows/**
Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/**
Docs/AI-Pipeline/**
AGENTS.md

When every mainline change is confined to those paths—and none overlaps the task’s changes or exclusive resources—the workflow:

Preserves your original PASS on its exact original commit.
Advances the Issue’s operational head to the new merge commit.
Records the old task commit, main commit, merge base, merge commit, changed paths, and classification in a hash-bound receipt.
Invalidates any old validation manifests, draft, proposal, or evidence tied to the pre-merge commit.
Returns to delivery_evidence.
Runs authoritative Unity tests against the new integrated commit.
Continues to the delivery-evidence proposal without asking you to repeat the same manual test.

The evidence will be accurate about the distinction:

Human-tested implementation commit:
5bf787a7...

Integrated commit being evidenced:
<new merge commit>

Human revalidation of integrated commit:
Not performed

Reason:
Main changed only allowlisted agent/CI/pipeline-documentation paths.

Authoritative Unity validation:
Must pass on the integrated commit.

It will not falsely claim that you manually tested the merge commit.

Runtime-sensitive mainline drift

Another human Unity handoff is mandatory if main changed:

Assets/**
Packages/**
ProjectSettings/**
Tasks/**
Docs/GDD/**
Docs/Engineering/**
Pipeline/Testing/**
Pipeline/TaskGraph/**
Pipeline/TaskDelivery/**

The same applies when:

An unknown, non-allowlisted path changed.
Main changed a file also changed by the task branch.
Main changed a task-exclusive resource.
The NSC-020 contract changed.
The merge changed the blob identity of one of the task’s implementation files.

In that case, the agent still creates and pushes the integration commit, but Issue #64 becomes:

human_action_required / unity_runtime_validation

The new handoff is bound to the integrated commit. The old PASS remains in the Issue history but does not authorize the new commit.

Fail-closed behavior

If Git reports a conflict, the transition:

git merge --abort
→ verifies original HEAD was restored
→ verifies checkout is clean
→ does not push
→ records a durable Issue blocker
→ stops

If whitespace or TaskGraph validation fails after the merge but before pushing, it resets to the original unpushed task head and records the blocker.

If the remote task branch moved unexpectedly, it refuses to overwrite it.

The previous repeated-action bug cannot recur here: blocked checkout preparation and no-progress actions now release or block the lease instead of repeatedly asking Codex to choose the same action.

& {
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

    $Repository = "C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\NoSafeCircle"
    $RequiredFeatureCommit = "c6705b34a9a2e9476e75882890a8d67cbce6dd48"

    Set-Location $Repository

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host "RUN GENERIC GAME TASK AGENT WITH REINTEGRATION" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host "================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host ""

    Write-Host "=== 1. VERIFY CLEAN CONTROLLER CHECKOUT ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    $DirtyPaths = @(git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all)

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not inspect the controller checkout."
    }

    if ($DirtyPaths.Count -gt 0) {
        git status --short --untracked-files=all
        throw "The controller checkout is dirty."
    }

    Write-Host "Controller checkout is clean." -ForegroundColor Green

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 2. UPDATE CONTROLLER MAIN ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    git switch main

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not switch the controller checkout to main."
    }

    git pull --ff-only origin main

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not update controller main."
    }

    $CurrentMain = (git rev-parse HEAD).Trim()
    $RemoteMain = (git rev-parse origin/main).Trim()

    Write-Host "Current main:"
    Write-Host "  $CurrentMain"
    Write-Host "Remote main:"
    Write-Host "  $RemoteMain"

    if ($CurrentMain -ne $RemoteMain) {
        throw "Local main does not equal origin/main."
    }

    git merge-base --is-ancestor $RequiredFeatureCommit $CurrentMain
    $FeatureAncestorExit = $LASTEXITCODE

    if ($FeatureAncestorExit -ne 0) {
        throw @"
The mainline-reintegration feature is not present.

Required commit:
$RequiredFeatureCommit

Current main:
$CurrentMain
"@
    }

    foreach ($RequiredPath in @(
        ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\mainline_reintegration.py",
        ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\tests\mainline_reintegration_smoke_test.py",
        ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1"
    )) {
        if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $RequiredPath -PathType Leaf)) {
            throw "Required reintegration file is missing: $RequiredPath"
        }
    }

    Write-Host "Mainline reintegration transition is present." -ForegroundColor Green

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 3. RUN LOCAL REINTEGRATION REGRESSION TESTS ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    python Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/mainline_reintegration_smoke_test.py

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "The local mainline reintegration regression test failed."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 4. VALIDATE TASKGRAPH ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    python Pipeline/TaskGraph/taskcontrol.py validate

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "TaskGraph validation failed."
    }

    $DirtyPaths = @(git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all)

    if ($DirtyPaths.Count -gt 0) {
        git status --short --untracked-files=all
        throw "Validation unexpectedly changed the controller checkout."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 5. RUN THE GENERIC GAME TASK AGENT ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "No task ID is supplied." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host "Issue #64 remains the durable resume token." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host "The agent should now select integrate_current_main." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host ""

    powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass `
        -File .\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1 `
        -ExecutionProvider claude `
        -MaxTurns 120

    $AgentExit = $LASTEXITCODE

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "Game Task Agent exit code:"
    Write-Host "  $AgentExit"

    if ($AgentExit -ne 0) {
        throw @"
The Game Task Agent stopped with exit code $AgentExit.

Do not manually merge or reset the NSC-020 checkout.
Preserve the terminal output and latest Issue #64 event.
"@
    }
}

43a5d5f...

└── c6705b3...   current main

That proves the agent integrated main into the task branch. It did not merge the task branch back into main.

There is also currently no NSC-020 pull request, so it could not have reached main.

Why it stopped

The run ended with:

status: human_revalidation_required
commit: 43a5d5f557dc8f46a91b0458757936e829f2af12
pull_request_url: null

Issue #64 is consequently in:

human_action_required / unity_runtime_validation

for the integrated commit. 

However, the reason it demanded another human test is a classifier bug.

The receipt says:

classification: runtime_sensitive

main_changed_paths:
  only .github/workflows/**
  and Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/**

task_blob_changes_after_merge:
  Assets/NoSafeCircle/DoorPrototype/Scenes/DoorPrototype.unity

Those findings contradict each other: the recorded main changes are entirely pipeline-related.

The exact false positive

The NSC-020 contract contains this exclusive resource:

Assets/NoSafeCircle/DoorPrototype/Scenes/DoorPrototype.unity

But the actual committed scene is:

Assets/Scenes/DoorPrototype.unity

The actual scene has the same Git blob SHA before and after reintegration:

Original tested commit 5bf787a...:
97fb8a066cf6bc184687ca1181b19c7e43c855d7

Integrated commit 43a5d5f...:
97fb8a066cf6bc184687ca1181b19c7e43c855d7

So the real scene did not change.

The classifier checks each path with:

git rev-parse <commit>:<path>

but does not use --verify. For a nonexistent path, Git writes the unresolved <commit>:<path> expression to stdout while also failing. Because the two commit prefixes differ, the code mistakenly concludes that the nonexistent file’s “blob identity” changed. That false result converts the otherwise automation_only integration into runtime_sensitive.

What this means

The correct classification should have been:

automation_only
human_revalidation_required: false

The pipeline should then have:

preserved your original human PASS
→ run fresh authoritative Unity tests on 43a5d5f...
→ prepared the delivery-evidence proposal
→ requested your evidence approval
→ created the NSC-020 PR
→ merged the task into main after checks

Instead, it created an unnecessary second human-testing handoff.

Do not retest commit 43a5d5f..., do not post another PASS, and do not rerun the generic agent yet. The next correct work is to fix the existence-aware blob comparison, add a regression for nonexistent exclusive-resource paths, correct the bad scene path in the task contract through the appropriate contract-change process, and repair Issue #64 so the integrated branch can resume delivery evidence without fabricating a second human validation.

    Write-Host "  $RemoteMain"

    if ($LocalMain -ne $RemoteMain) {
        throw "Local main does not equal origin/main."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 3. VERIFY REQUIRED MIGRATION FILES ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    foreach ($RequiredPath in @(
        ".\Pipeline\TaskGraph\scene_path_policy.py",
        ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\tests\scene_path_contract_migration_smoke_test.py",
        ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\tests\contract_migration_checkout_smoke_test.py",
        ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1"
    )) {
        if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $RequiredPath -PathType Leaf)) {
            throw "Required migration file is missing: $RequiredPath"
        }
    }

    Write-Host "All required files are present." -ForegroundColor Green

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 4. VALIDATE CANONICAL UNITY SCENE PATHS ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    python Pipeline/TaskGraph/scene_path_policy.py --json

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "The canonical Unity scene-path policy failed."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 5. RUN SCENE-PATH AND WORKFLOW REGRESSION ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    python Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/scene_path_contract_migration_smoke_test.py

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "The scene-path and contract-migration regression failed."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 6. RUN DURABLE CHECKOUT MIGRATION REGRESSION ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    python Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/contract_migration_checkout_smoke_test.py

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "The durable checkout contract-migration regression failed."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 7. VALIDATE TASKGRAPH ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    python Pipeline/TaskGraph/taskcontrol.py validate

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "TaskGraph validation failed."
    }

    $ControllerStatus = @(git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all)

    if ($ControllerStatus.Count -gt 0) {
        git status --short --untracked-files=all
        throw "Validation unexpectedly changed the controller checkout."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 8. VERIFY THE DURABLE ISSUE STATE ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    $QueueText = (
        & python Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/issue_queue.py `
            --source $Repository |
            Out-String
    )

    $QueueExit = $LASTEXITCODE

    Write-Host $QueueText

    if ($QueueExit -ne 0) {
        throw "The durable Issue queue could not be read."
    }

    try {
        $Queue = $QueueText | ConvertFrom-Json
    }
    catch {
        throw "The durable Issue queue did not return valid JSON."
    }

    $MatchingIssues = @(
        $Queue.agent_ready |
            Where-Object {
                $_.workflow_state.task_id -eq $TaskId
            }
    )

    if ($MatchingIssues.Count -ne 1) {
        throw "Expected exactly one agent-ready NSC-020 Issue, found $($MatchingIssues.Count)."
    }

    $WorkflowState = $MatchingIssues[0].workflow_state

    if ($WorkflowState.state -ne "agent_ready") {
        throw "NSC-020 is not agent_ready. Found: $($WorkflowState.state)"
    }

    if ($WorkflowState.phase -ne "delivery_evidence") {
        throw "NSC-020 is not in delivery_evidence. Found: $($WorkflowState.phase)"
    }

    if ($WorkflowState.task_contract_sha256 -ne $ExpectedContractHash) {
        throw @"
NSC-020 has the wrong task-contract identity.

Expected:
$ExpectedContractHash

Found:
$($WorkflowState.task_contract_sha256)
"@
    }

    if ($WorkflowState.human_handoff_commit -ne $ExpectedHumanTestedCommit) {
        throw @"
The preserved human-tested commit is wrong.

Expected:
$ExpectedHumanTestedCommit

Found:
$($WorkflowState.human_handoff_commit)
"@
    }

    if ($WorkflowState.human_result -ne "pass") {
        throw "The preserved human result is not PASS."
    }

    $RemoteTaskLines = @(
        git ls-remote origin "refs/heads/$TaskBranch"
    )

    $RemoteTaskExit = $LASTEXITCODE

    if ($RemoteTaskExit -ne 0 -or $RemoteTaskLines.Count -ne 1) {
        throw "Could not resolve the remote NSC-020 branch."
    }

    $RemoteTaskHead = (($RemoteTaskLines[0] -split "\s+")[0]).Trim()

    Write-Host "Issue operational head:"
    Write-Host "  $($WorkflowState.head_commit)"
    Write-Host "Remote task branch head:"
    Write-Host "  $RemoteTaskHead"
    Write-Host "Preserved human-tested commit:"
    Write-Host "  $($WorkflowState.human_handoff_commit)"
    Write-Host "Preserved human result:"
    Write-Host "  $($WorkflowState.human_result)"

    if ($WorkflowState.head_commit -ne $RemoteTaskHead) {
        throw "Issue #64 does not point to the remote NSC-020 branch head."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "NSC-020 durable migration state is valid." -ForegroundColor Green

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 9. RUN THE GENERIC GAME TASK AGENT ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "No task ID is supplied." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host "Issue #64 should be selected from delivery_evidence." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host "The original human PASS remains bound to 5bf787a7..." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host ""

    powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass `
        -File .\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1 `
        -ExecutionProvider claude `
        -MaxTurns 120

    $AgentExit = $LASTEXITCODE

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "Game Task Agent exit code:"
    Write-Host "  $AgentExit"

    if ($AgentExit -ne 0) {
        throw @"
The Game Task Agent stopped with exit code $AgentExit.

Do not manually reset, merge, or clean the NSC-020 checkout.
Preserve the terminal output, progress log, and latest Issue #64 event.
"@
    }
}

The local NSC-020 checkout may initially be reported as stale because it still has the prior contract identity. That is expected. The durable-checkout migration logic should fast-forward it to the remote task head, update its managed manifest, and then continue delivery evidence. At the current repository state, it should not request another manual Unity test merely because the scene path changed.

Verdict

Yes. I split the investigation into four independent review roles: workflow semantics, Git identity, failure recovery, and delivery-validation requirements.

They agree on the immediate cause, but they corrected my original fix in one important way:

The repository already supports preserving an old human PASS across an automation-only integration. The bug is that this support recognizes only a mainline_reintegration receipt, while NSC-020 was advanced through a task_contract_migrated event.

So my earlier proposal was directionally right, but “allow any ancestor PASS” would be too broad and unsafe. The right fix is to extend the existing receipt-based mechanism to verified clerical contract migrations.

The transcript proves the resulting deadlock: turns 10 through 14 invoke the same Unity action, receive the same exact-HEAD rejection, and make no durable progress. 

What each review found
1. Workflow-semantics review: the missing bridge is contract migration

The base downstream validator requires:

latest human result == PASS
human tested commit == checkout HEAD

Otherwise it raises:

exact human PASS for checkout HEAD is missing

That guard runs before authoritative Unity validation.

However, mainline_reintegration.py already patches this behavior. When it finds a valid automation_only reintegration receipt for the current head, it permits:

human-tested commit != operational HEAD

while still requiring that the original exact PASS exists. It then creates a truthful artifact distinguishing the human-tested implementation commit from the integrated commit being automatically validated.

NSC-020 did not reach 4f695cb... through that code path. It reached it through an append-only task_contract_migrated event that intentionally recorded:

Operational head:       4f695cb...
Human-tested commit:    5bf787a...
Human result:           PASS

The migration state is legal under the workflow contract, but no compatible reintegration receipt was added to downstream state. Therefore, the patched validator finds no recognized receipt and falls back to the old exact-HEAD rule.

Conclusion: adding another broad bypass is unnecessary. We should generalize the existing receipt system so a verified clerical contract migration produces the same kind of carry-forward authority as automation-only mainline reintegration.

2. Git-identity review: preserving the PASS is safe for this exact migration

The review compared the human-tested commit and the current operational commit directly.

These task-owned files have identical Git blob identities at both commits:

Task surface	Human-tested 5bf787a...	Operational 4f695cb...
DoorInteractable.cs	c4dbe234...	c4dbe234...
DoorInteractionPlayModeTests.cs	e073091...	e073091...
DoorPrototypeSceneBuilder.cs	ada666df...	ada666df...
Assets/Scenes/DoorPrototype.unity	97fb8a06...	97fb8a06...

The NSC-020 contract changed from revision 1 to revision 2. Its acceptance criteria and completion gate remained the same; the substantive change was the exclusive-resource scene path moving from the obsolete feature-folder path to Assets/Scenes/DoorPrototype.unity.

The committed migration ledger also identifies exactly that old-path-to-new-path replacement, along with the correct old and new contract hashes.

The merge commit itself says it advances the contract and canonical scene identity without changing the gameplay implementation.

Conclusion: for NSC-020 specifically, the original human PASS can safely remain attached to 5bf787a..., while fresh automated Unity validation runs against 4f695cb....

That does not mean all task-contract migrations are safe to carry forward automatically.

3. Safety review: accepting any ancestor PASS would be a mistake

The task_contract_migrated transition currently validates identities such as:

old and new contract hashes;
branch and operational head;
preserved human handoff commit;
preserved human result.

It does not itself prove that:

acceptance criteria stayed unchanged;
completion gates stayed unchanged;
no task-owned implementation file changed;
the operational commit contains only approved clerical changes;
the migration ledger matches the event.

Therefore, this would be unsafe:

if human_tested_commit_is_ancestor_of_head:
    accept_human_pass()

A future migration could alter actual behavioral requirements or task-owned code while still satisfying that ancestry check.

The carry-forward permission needs to be an independently verified, hash-bound receipt rather than an implication derived only from Git ancestry.

4. Failure-recovery review: there are two more loop defects

The existing GoalLoopGuard handles only prepare_task_checkout. It detects a checkout action that returns blocked or makes no progress, releases the lease, and makes the current run terminal.

It does not wrap arbitrary downstream actions.

The downstream loop handles deterministic action failure this way:

catch TaskReviewContractError
→ log action_rejected
→ append error to history
→ start next turn

It does not compare the new error with prior errors, does not detect unchanged workflow state, and does not release the lease after repeated rejection.

There is another asymmetry: the implementation pipeline tries to record a durable blocker when its turn budget is exhausted, while the downstream pipeline simply raises after exhausting its turns.

That produces three failure cases:

Repeated deterministic rejection loop, which is what happened here.
Maximum-turn failure while still holding the lease.
Ctrl+C or process crash while still holding the lease.

Issue #64 is currently still recorded as agent_working with the failed worker’s lease, so another generic agent cannot safely take it over yet.

One additional issue likely waiting behind this one

NSC-020’s completion gate does not explicitly name EditMode or PlayMode. The downstream code interprets completion-gate text and, when neither mode is found, defaults to requiring both platforms.

But the focused NSC-020 behavioral coverage is in DoorInteractionPlayModeTests, including:

opening does not set crossing state;
sealed-door crossing is ignored;
open-door forward crossing sets state and fires once;
unrelated colliders are ignored;
reset clears crossing state.

The Editor test directory contains scene-builder, camera-conformance, and build-scene-registration tests, not focused EditMode tests for NSC-020’s crossing behavior.

So after fixing the PASS receipt, the next run may still stop because it expects an EditMode manifest that the task contract never explicitly required.

That should be made explicit rather than guessed:

required_test_platforms:
  - PlayMode

or an equivalent contract-level field. If project policy genuinely requires an additional EditMode suite, it should name the exact authoritative filter.

Recommended fix
Part 1: Generalize the carry-forward receipt

Replace the special-purpose idea of “mainline reintegration receipt only” with a shared concept such as:

validated_human_pass_carry_forward

It may be produced by either:

automation-only mainline reintegration
verified clerical task-contract migration

For a contract migration, deterministic code should require all of the following:

The exact human PASS comment names the preserved human-tested commit.
The human-tested commit is an ancestor of the operational head.
The checkout branch and head exactly match the Issue.
The latest contiguous workflow history contains the matching task_contract_migrated event.
The migration event’s old/new hashes match the committed migration ledger.
Only an explicit allowlist of clerical contract fields changed.
Acceptance criteria, completion gates, dependencies, kind, scope, and decomposition semantics did not change.
Task-owned implementation, test, builder, prefab, and relevant scene blobs did not change.
Any old-resource-to-new-resource path replacement is explicitly declared in the migration ledger.
The resulting receipt is semantic-hashed and persisted in downstream state.

Then the existing patched guard can accept either receipt type.

Part 2: Preserve truthful evidence

The evidence should say:

Human-tested implementation commit:
5bf787a7f74aafd282ac770e4d736f4e7ea9e408

Operational commit under automated validation:
4f695cb67d8ff377be1946db6c99385672ddde86

Human result:
PASS for 5bf787a...

Human revalidation of operational commit:
NOT PERFORMED

Carry-forward authority:
verified clerical task-contract migration

Authoritative Unity validation:
performed against 4f695cb...

The existing mainline-reintegration artifact logic already follows this pattern, so this should be shared rather than reimplemented separately.

Part 3: Add a generic rejection circuit breaker

After an action rejection, fingerprint:

action
error type
normalized error
workflow state version
state
phase
next action
checkout head
checkout status

The run should terminate on the second identical rejection under the same state identity, or after a small number of differing rejections with no observation progress.

On termination it should:

append agent_lease_released
return Issue to agent_ready
record repeated_action_rejection
preserve the action/error fingerprint
finish the run as blocked

The same cleanup should run on downstream turn-budget exhaustion and, where safely possible, cancellation or interruption.

Part 4: Make test-platform authority explicit

Do not default silently to both EditMode and PlayMode. Require the task or delivery policy to identify the authoritative platform and exact filters.

For NSC-020, the evidence currently supports PlayMode as the task-specific authoritative platform.

Regression tests required before rerunning NSC-020

The current migration tests prove that the workflow event chain survives and that the checkout can fast-forward and rekey its manifest. They do not continue into _assert_human_tested_head or an authoritative Unity action.

The new suite should include:

Positive end-to-end contract migration: human PASS → clerical migration → checkout recovery → carry-forward receipt → mocked authoritative Unity PASS on operational head → delivery draft.
Behavioral contract change: acceptance criterion changes, so carry-forward is rejected.
Task-owned blob change: implementation or focused test changes, so carry-forward is rejected.
Ledger mismatch: migration event and committed ledger disagree, so carry-forward is rejected.
Non-ancestor operational head: rejected.
Forged or locally edited receipt: rejected by semantic hash.
Repeated action rejection: lease released after the second identical failure.
Turn exhaustion and interruption: no stranded lease.
Platform requirement: NSC-020 selects the intended PlayMode filter without inventing an unrelated EditMode obligation.
Final recommendation

Proceed with the refined fix. It should solve this exact NSC-020 failure and prevent the same class of failure on future clerical migrations.

Do not merely weaken the exact-commit guard to accept any ancestor PASS.

Do not rerun the generic agent until:

the carry-forward receipt supports verified contract migrations;
the generic rejection breaker exists;
NSC-020’s required test platform is explicit;
the current stale lease is released through a valid append-only workflow event.

My confidence in the diagnosis is high because the live Issue state, migration event, current source code, commit ancestry, and task-owned blob identities all line up. The final proof should be the new end-to-end regression followed by one controlled NSC-020 resume.

    $StatusExit = $LASTEXITCODE
    $StatusText = ($StatusOutput | Out-String).Trim()

    if ($StatusExit -ne 0) {
        Write-Host $StatusText
        throw "Could not inspect the controller checkout."
    }

    if (-not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($StatusText)) {
        Write-Host $StatusText
        throw "The controller checkout is dirty."
    }

    $SwitchOutput = & cmd.exe /d /s /c "git switch main 2>&1"
    $SwitchExit = $LASTEXITCODE
    $SwitchOutput | ForEach-Object { Write-Host $_ }

    if ($SwitchExit -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not switch the controller checkout to main."
    }

    $PullOutput = & cmd.exe /d /s /c `
        "git pull --ff-only origin main 2>&1"

    $PullExit = $LASTEXITCODE
    $PullOutput | ForEach-Object { Write-Host $_ }

    if ($PullExit -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not update controller main."
    }

    $LocalMainOutput = & cmd.exe /d /s /c "git rev-parse HEAD 2>&1"
    $LocalMainExit = $LASTEXITCODE
    $LocalMain = ($LocalMainOutput | Out-String).Trim()

    if ($LocalMainExit -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not resolve local main."
    }

    $RemoteMainOutput = & cmd.exe /d /s /c `
        "git rev-parse origin/main 2>&1"

    $RemoteMainExit = $LASTEXITCODE
    $RemoteMain = ($RemoteMainOutput | Out-String).Trim()

    if ($RemoteMainExit -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not resolve origin/main."
    }

    Write-Host "Local main:"
    Write-Host "  $LocalMain"
    Write-Host "Remote main:"
    Write-Host "  $RemoteMain"

    if ($LocalMain -ne $RemoteMain) {
        throw "Local main does not equal origin/main."
    }

    $AncestorOutput = & cmd.exe /d /s /c `
        "git merge-base --is-ancestor $RequiredFixCommit $LocalMain 2>&1"

    $AncestorExit = $LASTEXITCODE

    if ($AncestorExit -ne 0) {
        Write-Host $AncestorOutput
        throw @"
The merged downstream-reintegration fix is not present.

Required ancestor:
$RequiredFixCommit

Current main:
$LocalMain
"@
    }

        ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\authoritative_validation_policy.json",

    if ($QueueExit -ne 0) {

    try {

    $WorkflowState = $MatchingIssues[0].workflow_state

    if ($WorkflowState.phase -ne "delivery_evidence") {
        throw "NSC-020 is not in delivery_evidence."
    }

    if ($WorkflowState.human_result -ne "pass") {

    if ($null -ne $WorkflowState.lease_id) {

    }

    Write-Host ""
