    Write-Host "Operational head:"
    Write-Host "  $($WorkflowState.head_commit)"
    Write-Host "Preserved human-tested commit:"
    Write-Host "  $($WorkflowState.human_handoff_commit)"

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 6. RUN THE GENERIC GAME TASK AGENT ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "No task ID is supplied." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host "Issue #64 is selected from the durable queue." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host "The bridge is installed inside downstream_resilience.py." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host ""

    $PreviousErrorPreference = $ErrorActionPreference

    try {
        # Allow the child launcher to stream native Docker/GitHub stderr.
        # Its real exit code is checked immediately afterward.
        $ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"

        powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass `
            -File .\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1 `
            -ExecutionProvider claude `
            -MaxTurns 120

        $AgentExit = $LASTEXITCODE
    }
    finally {
        $ErrorActionPreference = $PreviousErrorPreference
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "Game Task Agent exit code:"
    Write-Host "  $AgentExit"

    if ($AgentExit -ne 0) {
        throw @"
The Game Task Agent stopped with exit code $AgentExit.

Do not manually reset, merge, or clean the NSC-020 checkout.
Preserve the terminal output, progress log, and latest Issue #64 event.
"@
    }

* * *

**ChatGPT:**

Yes. Your impression is correct: the useful debug output has regressed, and the error is exposing a real control-flow problem—not a Unity failure, but not something we should normalize as harmless either.

What that error means

This line:

action=search_repository
error_type=DownstreamPipelineError
error=repository prefix must be non-empty

means Codex selected the repository-search tool but supplied at least one blank or whitespace-only entry in its prefixes argument. The host rejected the call before searching or modifying anything.

The run then continued normally to another turn. On the next attempt, it supplied a nonempty but unauthorized prefix:

repository prefix is outside downstream read roots

and then began Turn 8. So the first error did not crash the process or damage the task. 

The downstream reader currently permits only:

Assets/
Tasks/
Docs/GDD/
Docs/Engineering/
Docs/AI-Pipeline/UNITY_PROGRAMMER_LANGUAGE.md

It rejects general repository roots and Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/.

Why the agent is searching at all

This is the more important defect.

The deterministic state repeatedly says:

next_action=run_authoritative_unity_tests
checkout_status=ready

The new resilience layer also injects the exact authoritative plan:

Platform:
PlayMode

Filter:
NoSafeCircle.DoorPrototype.Tests.DoorInteractionPlayModeTests

and explicitly tells Codex not to search for or infer another filter.

But the older supervisor instructions still say:

Read the Issue log, task contract, Unity testing policy,
and programmer-language policy.

Codex is following that older instruction and wandering around trying to locate policies, despite already having the exact deterministic test plan. That is why it keeps choosing:

list_repository_files
search_repository
read_repository_file

instead of running the test.

This is not yet the old identical-action loop. It is supervisor wandering: the workflow state is stable and valid, but the model is wasting turns on unnecessary reads and occasionally malformed arguments.

Why the rejection guard did not fire

The new circuit breaker counts repeated rejections by a fingerprint that includes:

action
exact normalized error
workflow state version
phase
next action
checkout status
checkout head

Your two errors differed:

repository prefix must be non-empty
repository prefix is outside downstream read roots

Therefore, they had different fingerprints. The “second identical rejection” breaker correctly did not consider them identical.

That reveals another gap: we also need a same-state rejection streak. For example, three different rejected actions under the same state version, checkout head, and deterministic next action should terminate the run even when their error strings differ.

Why less debug information is visible

The terminal logger prints only scalar fields. Nested objects are deliberately omitted from terminal lines:

if isinstance(value, _SCALAR)

The result_summary passed to action_completed is a dictionary, so it is stored in progress.jsonl but does not appear in the terminal or progress.log.

More importantly, supervisor_decision logs:

action
rationale

but not the action arguments.

That is why we can see:

Codex selected search_repository

but cannot see:

query=...
prefixes=[...]

We know one prefix was empty because of deterministic validation, but we cannot see the exact submitted argument. The durable structured log does not solve that because the decision arguments were never included in the progress event.

So yes: the logging is alive, but it is too aggressively summarized to diagnose malformed tool calls.

What to do with the current run

Issue #64 still shows:

state: agent_working
phase: delivery_evidence
worker: task-review-agent-desktop-6lmnlck-e37f47ba91
head: 4f695cb67d8ff377be1946db6c99385672ddde86

The lease remains active, which means the run has not durably completed or released itself yet.

At the point captured in your transcript, Turn 8 was still choosing an action. Use this rule:

If the next decision is:
run_authoritative_unity_test
with:
PlayMode
NoSafeCircle.DoorPrototype.Tests.DoorInteractionPlayModeTests
let it continue.
If it chooses another search_repository, list_repository_files, or policy-reading detour, press:
Ctrl+C
The interruption handler should append a lease-release event and return Issue #64 to agent_ready.

Do not start a second agent while Issue #64 still says agent_working.

The correction we should make

The next patch should address all of this together:

Deterministic action routing. When next_action=run_authoritative_unity_tests and an exact authoritative_test_plan exists, offer Codex only the authorized Unity action—not generic repository browsing.
Remove contradictory instructions. Do not require Codex to search for a Unity policy after host code has already loaded and validated it.
Print sanitized action arguments. A decision should visibly show:
action=search_repository
query="..."
prefixes=["..."]
with bounded lengths and no file contents or credentials.
Flatten result summaries. Instead of hiding the nested dictionary:
result_summary={"status":"ready","head_commit":"..."}
print:
result_status=ready
result_head_commit=...
Add a same-state rejection breaker. Stop after a small number of different rejections when the workflow state, checkout identity, and deterministic next action have not changed.
Harden schemas and optional arguments. Blank prefixes should be impossible in the structured schema, and nullable optional arguments should be removed before calling functions so None cannot override a safe default.

The current search error did not corrupt anything. But your larger concern is valid: the agent has enough deterministic information to run the exact test, yet the supervisor is still allowed to wander, and the screen does not show enough detail to explain its arguments.

**ChatGPT:**

Codex kept browsing while the deterministic next action was already run_authoritative_unity_tests, including malformed repository-prefix calls.
When it finally invoked the test action, the host rejected it with no unique task-contract migration authorizes the preserved human PASS, after which the state changed toward integrate_current_main. 

For this review, use Claude Fable 5. Anthropic currently describes claude-fable-5 as its most capable broadly released model. Claude Docs (https://docs.anthropic.com/it/docs/about-claude/models/migrating-to-claude-4?utm_source=chatgpt.com) The repository’s regular Claude ArchitectureReview currently defaults to claude-sonnet-5, so the command below explicitly requests Fable 5 instead.

This is a targeted review, not the broad eight-agent ArchitectureReview. It uses the existing claude-review Docker service, where the repository is read-only and only Pipeline/ArchitectureReview/outputs/ is writable.

Paste this complete PowerShell block
& {
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

    $Repository = "C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\NoSafeCircle"
    $AgentOutputRoot = "C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\.task-review-agent\outputs\NSC-020"

    # Anthropic's strongest broadly available model.
    $Model = "claude-fable-5"

    Set-Location $Repository

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host "TARGETED CLAUDE REVIEW OF TASK AGENT FAILURE" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host "================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host ""

    Write-Host "=== 1. VERIFY CLEAN CURRENT MAIN ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    git switch main

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not switch to main."
    }

    git pull --ff-only origin main

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not update main."
    }

    $LocalHead = (git rev-parse HEAD).Trim()
    $RemoteHead = (git rev-parse origin/main).Trim()

    if ($LocalHead -ne $RemoteHead) {
        throw "Local main does not equal origin/main."
    }

    $Status = @(git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all)

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not inspect the controller checkout."
    }

    if ($Status.Count -gt 0) {
        git status --short --untracked-files=all
        throw "The controller checkout is dirty."
    }

    Write-Host "Frozen review commit:"
    Write-Host "  $LocalHead"
    Write-Host "Controller checkout is clean." -ForegroundColor Green

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 2. CAPTURE THE LATEST NSC-020 RUN LOG ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $AgentOutputRoot -PathType Container)) {
        throw "The NSC-020 durable output directory does not exist: $AgentOutputRoot"
    }

    $LatestProgress = Get-ChildItem `
        -LiteralPath $AgentOutputRoot `
        -Recurse `
        -File `
        -Filter "progress.log" |
        Sort-Object LastWriteTimeUtc -Descending |
        Select-Object -First 1

    if ($null -eq $LatestProgress) {
        throw "No NSC-020 progress.log was found."
    }

    $SourceRunDirectory = $LatestProgress.Directory

    Write-Host "Latest Game Task Agent run:"
    Write-Host "  $($SourceRunDirectory.FullName)"

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 3. CREATE THE TARGETED REVIEW PACKAGE ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    $ReviewRunId = (
        "nsc020-downstream-debug-" +
        (Get-Date).ToUniversalTime().ToString("yyyyMMdd-HHmmss")
    )

    $RelativeOutputDirectory = (
        "Pipeline/ArchitectureReview/outputs/targeted/" +
        $ReviewRunId
    )

    $OutputDirectory = Join-Path `
        $Repository `
        ($RelativeOutputDirectory -replace "/", "\")

    New-Item `
        -ItemType Directory `
        -Path $OutputDirectory `
        -ErrorAction Stop |
        Out-Null

    Copy-Item `
        -LiteralPath $LatestProgress.FullName `
        -Destination (Join-Path $OutputDirectory "incident-progress.log")

    foreach ($OptionalArtifact in @(
        "progress.jsonl",
        "run.json"
    )) {
        $SourceArtifact = Join-Path `
            $SourceRunDirectory.FullName `
            $OptionalArtifact

        if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $SourceArtifact -PathType Leaf) {
            Copy-Item `
                -LiteralPath $SourceArtifact `
                -Destination (Join-Path $OutputDirectory $OptionalArtifact)
        }
    }

    $PromptPath = Join-Path $OutputDirectory "prompt.txt"
    $ScriptPath = Join-Path $OutputDirectory "run-claude-review.sh"
    $ResponsePath = Join-Path $OutputDirectory "claude-response.json"

    $ContainerOutputDirectory = (
        "/workspace/" +
        $RelativeOutputDirectory
    )

    $ContainerPromptPath = "$ContainerOutputDirectory/prompt.txt"
    $ContainerResponsePath = "$ContainerOutputDirectory/claude-response.json"

    $Prompt = @"

No Safe Circle autonomous Unity-development pipeline.

the current implementation, prior diagnoses, Issue state, or proposed fixes

Repository commit:
$LocalHead

PRIMARY INCIDENT LOG

$RelativeOutputDirectory/incident-progress.log

When present, also inspect:

$RelativeOutputDirectory/progress.jsonl
$RelativeOutputDirectory/run.json

The operator also reports that earlier versions printed more useful debugging

   already contained an exact authoritative Unity platform and filter.

   action set for that state, or merely discouraged through prompting.

4. Determine why the host reported:

6. Review these proposed changes critically:

    $Utf8NoBom = New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false)

        ($Prompt -replace "`r`n", "`n") -replace "`r", "`n"

        $PromptPath,

    $BashScript = @"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

claude -p \
  --safe-mode \
  --model '$Model' \
  --max-turns 80 \
  --permission-mode dontAsk \
  --input-format text \
  --output-format json \
  --no-session-persistence \
  --setting-sources user,project \
  --tools Read,Glob,Grep \
  --allowedTools Read Glob Grep \
  --disallowedTools Bash,Edit,Write,NotebookEdit,WebSearch,WebFetch \
  < '$ContainerPromptPath' \
  > '$ContainerResponsePath'
"@

        ($BashScript -replace "`r`n", "`n") -replace "`r", "`n"
    )

        $ScriptPath,
        $BashScriptLf,
        $Utf8NoBom
    )

    Write-Host "  $OutputDirectory"
    Write-Host "Model:"
    Write-Host "  $Model"

    Write-Host "Claude cannot Bash, edit, write, browse the web, or modify GitHub." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host ""

    $PreviousErrorPreference = $ErrorActionPreference

    try {
        # Docker writes ordinary container lifecycle information to stderr.
        # Inspect the real process exit code instead of treating that output
        # as a PowerShell exception.
        $ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"

        docker compose -p nosafecircle run --rm -T `
            claude-review `
            bash "$ContainerOutputDirectory/run-claude-review.sh"

        $ClaudeExit = $LASTEXITCODE
    }
    finally {
        $ErrorActionPreference = $PreviousErrorPreference
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "Claude review exit code:"
    Write-Host "  $ClaudeExit"

    if ($ClaudeExit -ne 0) {
        throw @"
The targeted Claude review failed with exit code $ClaudeExit.

The prompt and incident package remain at:
$OutputDirectory

The requested model was:
$Model

If your Claude account does not expose that model, change only:
`$Model = "claude-opus-5"
and run the block again with a new review directory.
"@
    }

    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $ResponsePath -PathType Leaf)) {
        throw "Claude exited successfully but did not create claude-response.json."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "================================================" -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "CLAUDE REVIEW COMPLETE" -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "================================================" -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "Response:"
    Write-Host "  $ResponsePath"
    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "Complete review package:"
    Write-Host "  $OutputDirectory"
    Write-Host ""

    $Envelope = Get-Content `
        -LiteralPath $ResponsePath `
        -Raw `
        -Encoding UTF8 |
        ConvertFrom-Json

    if ($null -ne $Envelope.result) {
        Write-Host "---------------- CLAUDE REVIEW ----------------"
        Write-Host $Envelope.result
        Write-Host "------------------------------------------------"
    }
    else {
        Get-Content `
            -LiteralPath $ResponsePath `
            -Raw `
            -Encoding UTF8 |
            Write-Host
    }
}

The presentation layer is separate from workflow authority; it changes how operations are explained and logged, not what the agent is permitted to do.

Malformed action arguments are now visible

[BLOCKED] Turn 6: Blocked while trying to search project code and documentation.

For an out-of-scope search, it will explain:

That is much more useful than leaving you to infer what repository prefix means.

Every run now creates:

The concise operator view. It matches what appears in PowerShell and uses Unity-oriented wording.

debug.log

Every technical lifecycle event, including events removed from the normal display:

state_observation_started

codex_supervisor_completed
pipeline_action_completed
pipeline_action_failed

This is the file to send when something still behaves strangely.

progress.jsonl

The complete structured append-only journal. It retains sequence numbers, timestamps, stages, sanitized arguments, usage counters, safe result fields, and original technical messages.

The implementation now explicitly creates the operator, debug, and structured views while flattening safe nested fields for the screen.

Operator versus debug terminal mode

Normal behavior is:

$env:NSC_TASK_AGENT_LOG_VERBOSITY = "operator"

That suppresses duplicate low-level events from the terminal while preserving them in debug.log.

For a controlled diagnostic run, use:

$env:NSC_TASK_AGENT_LOG_VERBOSITY = "debug"

That prints the technical event names on screen as well.

The default is operator, so you normally do not need to set anything.

Update and verify the logging fix

This block updates main, verifies that the logging merge is present, runs the focused logging test, and validates TaskGraph. It deliberately does not restart NSC-020.

& {
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

    $Repository = "C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\NoSafeCircle"
    $RequiredLoggingCommit = "8f0330277eef2b9ec994494b1e5315cb7c739aec"

    Set-Location $Repository

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host "UPDATE AND VERIFY UNITY-FRIENDLY AGENT LOGGING" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host "================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host ""

    Write-Host "=== 1. VERIFY CLEAN CONTROLLER CHECKOUT ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    $Status = @(git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all)

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not inspect the controller checkout."
    }

    if ($Status.Count -gt 0) {
        git status --short --untracked-files=all
        throw "The controller checkout is dirty."
    }

    Write-Host "Controller checkout is clean." -ForegroundColor Green

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 2. UPDATE MAIN ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    git switch main

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not switch to main."
    }

    git pull --ff-only origin main

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not update main."
    }

    $LocalMain = (git rev-parse HEAD).Trim()
    $RemoteMain = (git rev-parse origin/main).Trim()

    Write-Host "Local main:"
    Write-Host "  $LocalMain"

    Write-Host "Remote main:"
    Write-Host "  $RemoteMain"

    if ($LocalMain -ne $RemoteMain) {
        throw "Local main does not equal origin/main."
    }

    git merge-base --is-ancestor $RequiredLoggingCommit $LocalMain

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw @"
The Unity-friendly logging fix is not present.

Required ancestor:
$RequiredLoggingCommit

Current main:
$LocalMain
"@
    }

    Write-Host "Unity-friendly logging fix is present." -ForegroundColor Green

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 3. VERIFY LOGGING FILES ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    foreach ($RequiredPath in @(
        ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\progress.py",
        ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\operator_logging.py",
        ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\tests\progress_logging_smoke_test.py"
    )) {
        if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $RequiredPath -PathType Leaf)) {
            throw "Required logging file is missing: $RequiredPath"
        }
    }

    Write-Host "Required logging files are present." -ForegroundColor Green

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 4. RUN LOGGING REGRESSION TESTS ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    python Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/progress_logging_smoke_test.py

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "The TaskReviewAgent logging regression test failed."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 5. VALIDATE TASKGRAPH ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    python Pipeline/TaskGraph/taskcontrol.py validate

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "TaskGraph validation failed."
    }

    $Status = @(git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all)

    if ($Status.Count -gt 0) {
        git status --short --untracked-files=all
        throw "Validation unexpectedly changed the controller checkout."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "Logging verification complete." -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "Default future view: operator" -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "Full technical events will also be written to debug.log." -ForegroundColor Green
}

agent_ready / delivery_evidence
lease_id: null

**ChatGPT:**

I reviewed the Claude package

It was a useful review. Claude Fable 5 completed 39 turns in roughly 11½ minutes, with no provider error. This was a single deep review, not a multi-agent panel—the package reports zero spawned subagents. 

Claude correctly identified several structural problems:

The supervisor is explicitly told to read policies that its repository boundary prevents it from reading.
The deterministic router says Unity testing is next while still allowing unrelated browsing actions.
Important carry-forward authority exists only in the machine-local downstream state file.
The rejection breaker only recognizes identical failures, not several different failures under the same unchanged state.
Full action results are repeatedly fed back into later prompts, causing major prompt growth and increasingly slow Codex decisions.
next_action values and executable action names are inconsistent.
An empty prefixes array can bypass the intended search-root restriction.

However, Claude’s headline explanation for the immediate migration failure is incomplete.

I found the direct cause of the current failure

Claude suspected this:

The task-contract migration event no longer matches because the operational head advanced beyond the event’s recorded head.

That is not what happened in the current Issue.

The migration event records:

Operational head:
4f695cb67d8ff377be1946db6c99385672ddde86

Original human-tested commit:
5bf787a7f74aafd282ac770e4d736f4e7ea9e408

New task-contract hash:
f8c9e326646e16e2c4bcf5eba4a6505494a5044491bc70127d5b0a1603150a3b

Issue #64 currently contains those same identities.

The migration matcher requires exactly those values:

details["head_commit"] == current_head
details["human_handoff_commit"] == human["tested_commit"]
details["human_result"] == "pass"
details["new_task_contract_sha256"] == current_contract_hash

The head and contract hash match. The failure is the value returned as human["tested_commit"].

The pipeline mistakes an agent-written example for your real test result

The actual human PASS comment says:

Result: PASS
Tested commit: 5bf787a7f...

Later, the agent posted the false revalidation handoff for 43a5d5f.... That agent-authored handoff included this instructional template:

## Human validation result

Result: PASS
Tested commit: `43a5d5f557dc8f46a91b0458757936e829f2af12`

Completed steps:
- ...

The downstream function _latest_human_validation() reads all Issue comments in reverse order, parses the first comment containing Result: PASS and Tested commit:, and does not check:

who authored the comment;
whether it is a workflow handoff;
whether the text is inside an instructional code block;
whether a validated human_validation_passed event authorizes it.

The parser itself is intentionally simple: its regular expression searches anywhere in the comment body for those two lines.

Therefore, the pipeline most likely does this:

Real human PASS:
5bf787a7...

Later agent handoff template:
43a5d5f5...

_latest_human_validation():
returns 43a5d5f5...

Migration event:
preserves 5bf787a7...

Matcher:
43a5d5f5 != 5bf787a7

Result:
“no unique task-contract migration authorizes the preserved human PASS”

This exactly fits the observed error.

It is also more serious than an ordinary parsing bug: agent-authored prose is being treated as human validation authority.

What Claude got completely right
The prompt creates the browsing loop

The downstream supervisor is told:

Read the Issue log, task contract, Unity testing policy,
and programmer-language policy.

But downstream repository access permits only:

Assets/
Tasks/
Docs/GDD/
Docs/Engineering/
Docs/AI-Pipeline/UNITY_PROGRAMMER_LANGUAGE.md

The authoritative validation policy lives under Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/, which is not readable through that boundary.

That directly explains why Codex kept trying malformed list_repository_files and search_repository calls. It was trying to satisfy a host instruction that the host made impossible.

The Issue is not yet the complete resume token

Automation-only integration authority is currently recovered only from:

controller.state["mainline_reintegration"]

That is a machine-local JSON file. _automation_receipt_for() does not reconstruct the receipt from the append-only Issue events.

So Claude is right that a different machine—or a deleted/corrupted local state file—can lose valid carry-forward authority even when the Issue contains enough durable information to reconstruct and verify it.

Routing is inconsistent

The deterministic pipeline emits:

run_authoritative_unity_tests

but the executable action is:

run_authoritative_unity_test

It also emits values such as:

verify_delivery_approval
finalize_delivery_evidence
open_pull_request

that are not direct supervisor actions.

That prevents strict deterministic action narrowing until the vocabulary is normalized.

Empty search prefixes are unsafe

The runtime validates each supplied prefix but does not require the prefix list itself to contain anything:

approved = [_safe_prefix(value) for value in prefixes]
git grep ... HEAD -- *approved

With prefixes=[], Git receives no restricting pathspec.

That is a real scope bug and should be fixed regardless of NSC-020.

What the logging fix already handled

Claude recommended:

showing sanitized action arguments;
flattening result summaries;
exposing Unity platform and filter;
displaying provider usage;
providing a concise operator view plus a complete technical view.

Those changes are already merged through PR #74 at:

8f0330277eef2b9ec994494b1e5315cb7c739aec

The next controlled run will show the malformed argument directly, for example:

arg_prefixes=<blank>, Docs/Engineering/

and will produce both:

progress.log   # readable operator output
debug.log      # every technical event
progress.jsonl # structured journal

That improves diagnosis, but it does not fix the control flow.

Correct patch order

I would not implement Claude’s plan verbatim. The correct order is:

1. Make validated workflow events authoritative for human results

_latest_human_validation() should stop treating arbitrary comments as evidence.

It should derive authority from the validated append-only event chain:

human_validation_passed
human_validation_failed

The event already contains:

tested_commit
result
human_comment_sha256
actor_id

The original comment may still be retrieved for the human-validation artifact, but only when its bytes match the event’s recorded hash. Agent handoff comments, examples, quoted text, and code blocks must never become human authority.

Required regression:

real human PASS for 5bf...
→ later agent handoff contains example PASS for 43a...
→ latest authoritative result remains 5bf...

This should be the first fix because it directly explains the current failure.

2. Narrow actions deterministically

When the host observation says:

next_action=run_authoritative_unity_test
authoritative_test_plan.platform=PlayMode
authoritative_test_plan.filter=DoorInteractionPlayModeTests

the supervisor should receive only a small relevant action set, such as:

run_authoritative_unity_test
prepare_task_checkout
read_issue_log

It should not receive general repository search actions at that state.

3. Remove the contradictory policy-discovery instruction

The supervisor should be told:

The host has already loaded and validated the authoritative Unity policy.
Use the exact platform and filter in downstream.authoritative_test_plan.
Do not search for another policy or infer another test filter.
4. Reconstruct carry-forward receipts from durable Issue events

Claude’s receipt concern remains valid even after fixing the comment parser.

A missing local downstream JSON file should trigger:

read validated integration/migration Issue events
→ verify event hashes
→ verify Git ancestry and merge parents
→ verify task-owned blobs
→ reconstruct receipt
→ persist local cache

The local file should be a cache, not the only authority.

5. Fix stale-checkout routing

main_commit_unavailable and origin_main_refresh_required should route directly to:

prepare_task_checkout

The agent should not attempt Unity validation until the checkout has the Git object needed to decide whether mainline integration is current.

6. Add general no-progress protection

In addition to the identical-error fingerprint, stop after several different rejections under the same:

state_version
phase
next_action
checkout_head

That would have stopped the browsing sequence before wasting most of the run.

Also:

require at least one nonblank repository prefix;
add schema minLength;
store only bounded summaries in supervisor history;
align all next_action names with executable actions.
Current status

Issue #64 is safe:

state: agent_ready
phase: delivery_evidence
head: 4f695cb67d8ff377be1946db6c99385672ddde86
human-tested commit: 5bf787a7f74aafd282ac770e4d736f4e7ea9e408
human result: pass
lease: none

Do not run the generic agent again yet. It would still encounter the comment-authority defect, although the new logging would make the failure much easier to understand.

Fixed and merged

The uploaded incident package showed the two central failures clearly: the agent kept browsing while deterministic state already required Unity validation, then rejected the test because it could not match the preserved human PASS to the contract migration. 

I implemented the corrections as PR #75 — “Make downstream human authority and routing deterministic.” The PR is merged, and main is now:

165f42b2dc21c300600e6566fe743eb68056b466
What was fixed
Human Unity results now come from durable authority

The pipeline no longer scans arbitrary Issue comments backward and accepts the first text containing:

Result: PASS
Tested commit: ...

That was dangerous because an agent-authored handoff included a copyable PASS template for 43a5d5f..., which could outrank your genuine PASS for 5bf787a....

The new resolution requires all three identities to agree:

validated human_validation_passed event
+
event’s human_comment_sha256
+
the exact original human comment bytes

An agent template, quotation, code block, workflow instruction, or copied example can no longer become human validation authority. The regression test explicitly places an agent-written fake PASS after the genuine human PASS and confirms that the genuine commit remains authoritative.

Exact Unity-test states no longer expose browsing actions

When the host has already established:

Platform:
PlayMode

Filter:
NoSafeCircle.DoorPrototype.Tests.DoorInteractionPlayModeTests

Codex is now offered only:

run_authoritative_unity_test

It is no longer offered:

read_issue_log
list_repository_files
search_repository
read_repository_file

in that state.

The contradictory supervisor instruction to find and read another Unity policy was also removed. The host-loaded, committed validation policy is authoritative.

The action vocabulary is normalized

The deterministic state and executable action now agree:

run_authoritative_unity_test

rather than mixing:

run_authoritative_unity_tests
run_authoritative_unity_test

The same normalization covers downstream finalization actions that previously had different planning and execution names.

Integration receipts can be recovered from the Issue

Automation-only carry-forward authority is no longer available only from the machine-local downstream JSON file.

When that local cache is missing, the pipeline can reconstruct it from:

append-only Issue integration event
+
ordered Git merge parents
+
current Git diffs
+
automation-only path classification
+
unchanged task-owned blob identities

A reconstructed receipt is accepted only after those identities are reverified. A test proves that changing a task-owned implementation blob makes reconstruction fail closed.

Stale Git information routes to checkout preparation

These states now route directly to:

prepare_task_checkout

before Unity validation:

origin_main_refresh_required
main_commit_unavailable

The agent should no longer try a Unity test first merely to discover that its local origin/main data was stale.

Multiple different failures can no longer wander indefinitely

The existing breaker handled the same identical rejection twice. The new breaker also watches the broader deterministic state identity:

state version
workflow state
phase
next action
checkout status
checkout HEAD

After three rejected actions without any change to that identity, it:

releases the lease
returns the Issue to agent_ready
records the rejection streak
terminates the run as blocked

So a sequence like:

blank search prefix
→ out-of-scope search prefix
→ another malformed read

will not consume the remaining turn budget merely because the individual error strings differ.

Repository searches are fail-closed

These are now rejected before git grep can run:

prefixes=[]
prefixes=[""]
prefixes=["Assets/", "   "]
prefixes supplied as a plain string

At least one nonblank, authorized repository prefix is required.

Supervisor history is bounded

Full Issue logs and repository file contents are no longer copied into every later Codex prompt.

History now retains bounded facts such as:

turn
action
short rationale
result status
commit/run/plan IDs
safe item counts
normalized error

This should prevent the escalating prompt size and increasingly slow decision turns seen near the end of earlier runs.

Close Unity first. Then paste this complete block into PowerShell:

& {
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

    $Repository = "C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\NoSafeCircle"
    $RequiredFixCommit = "165f42b2dc21c300600e6566fe743eb68056b466"

    Set-Location $Repository

    $env:PYTHONUTF8 = "1"
    $env:NSC_TASK_AGENT_LOG_VERBOSITY = "operator"

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host "UPDATE MAIN AND CONTINUE DURABLE TASK WORK" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host "================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host ""

    Write-Host "=== 1. VERIFY CLEAN CONTROLLER CHECKOUT ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    $StatusOutput = & cmd.exe /d /s /c `
        "git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all 2>&1"

    $StatusExit = $LASTEXITCODE
    $StatusText = ($StatusOutput | Out-String).Trim()

    if ($StatusExit -ne 0) {
        Write-Host $StatusText
        throw "Could not inspect the controller checkout."
    }

    if (-not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($StatusText)) {
        Write-Host $StatusText
        throw "The controller checkout is dirty."
    }

    Write-Host "Controller checkout is clean." -ForegroundColor Green

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 2. UPDATE CONTROLLER MAIN ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    git switch main

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not switch to main."
    }

    git pull --ff-only origin main

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not update main."
    }

    $LocalMain = (git rev-parse HEAD).Trim()
    $RemoteMain = (git rev-parse origin/main).Trim()

    Write-Host "Local main:"
    Write-Host "  $LocalMain"

    Write-Host "Remote main:"
    Write-Host "  $RemoteMain"

    if ($LocalMain -ne $RemoteMain) {
        throw "Local main does not equal origin/main."
    }

    git merge-base --is-ancestor $RequiredFixCommit $LocalMain

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw @"
The downstream authority/routing fix is not present.

Required ancestor:
$RequiredFixCommit

Current main:
$LocalMain
"@
    }

    Write-Host "Downstream authority/routing fix is present." -ForegroundColor Green

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 3. VERIFY REQUIRED FILES ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    foreach ($RequiredPath in @(
        ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\downstream_determinism.py",
        ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\downstream_resilience.py",
        ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\operator_logging.py",
        ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\tests\downstream_determinism_smoke_test.py",
        ".\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\tests\downstream_resilience_smoke_test.py"
    )) {
        if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $RequiredPath -PathType Leaf)) {
            throw "Required merged file is missing: $RequiredPath"
        }
    }

    Write-Host "Required merged files are present." -ForegroundColor Green

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 4. RUN TARGETED DOWNSTREAM REGRESSIONS ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    python Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/downstream_determinism_smoke_test.py

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "The downstream determinism regression suite failed."
    }

    python Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/downstream_resilience_smoke_test.py

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "The downstream resilience regression suite failed."
    }

    python Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/progress_logging_smoke_test.py

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "The operator logging regression suite failed."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 5. VALIDATE TASKGRAPH ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    python Pipeline/TaskGraph/taskcontrol.py validate

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "TaskGraph validation failed."
    }

    $StatusOutput = & cmd.exe /d /s /c `
        "git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all 2>&1"

    $StatusExit = $LASTEXITCODE
    $StatusText = ($StatusOutput | Out-String).Trim()

    if ($StatusExit -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not reinspect the controller checkout."
    }

    if (-not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($StatusText)) {
        Write-Host $StatusText
        throw "Validation unexpectedly changed the controller checkout."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 6. VERIFY NSC-020 IS RESUMABLE ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    $QueueText = (
        & python Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/issue_queue.py `
            --source $Repository |
            Out-String
    ).Trim()

    $QueueExit = $LASTEXITCODE

    Write-Host $QueueText

    if ($QueueExit -ne 0) {
        throw "The durable Issue queue could not be read."
    }

    try {
        $Queue = $QueueText | ConvertFrom-Json
    }
    catch {
        throw "The durable Issue queue did not return valid JSON."
    }

    $MatchingIssues = @(
        $Queue.agent_ready |
            Where-Object {
                $_.workflow_state.task_id -eq "NSC-020"
            }
    )

    if ($MatchingIssues.Count -ne 1) {
        throw @"
Expected exactly one agent-ready NSC-020 Issue.

Found:
$($MatchingIssues.Count)
"@
    }

    $WorkflowState = $MatchingIssues[0].workflow_state

    if ($WorkflowState.phase -ne "delivery_evidence") {
        throw "NSC-020 is not in delivery_evidence."
    }

    if ($WorkflowState.human_result -ne "pass") {
        throw "NSC-020 does not preserve the original human PASS."
    }

    if ($null -ne $WorkflowState.lease_id) {
        throw "NSC-020 unexpectedly has an active lease."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "Issue #64 is ready." -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "Operational head:"
    Write-Host "  $($WorkflowState.head_commit)"
    Write-Host "Human-tested commit:"
    Write-Host "  $($WorkflowState.human_handoff_commit)"
    Write-Host "Human result:"
    Write-Host "  $($WorkflowState.human_result)"

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 7. RUN THE GENERIC GAME TASK AGENT ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "No task ID is supplied." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host "The durable Issue queue chooses the work." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host "Exact Unity-test states will not expose repository browsing." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host ""

    $PreviousErrorPreference = $ErrorActionPreference

    try {
        # Let Docker/GitHub native stderr stream normally. The real child
        # process exit code is inspected immediately afterward.
        $ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"

        powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass `
            -File .\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1 `
            -ExecutionProvider claude `
            -MaxTurns 120

        $AgentExit = $LASTEXITCODE
    }
    finally {
        $ErrorActionPreference = $PreviousErrorPreference
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "Game Task Agent exit code:"
    Write-Host "  $AgentExit"

    if ($AgentExit -ne 0) {
        throw @"
The Game Task Agent stopped with exit code $AgentExit.

Do not manually reset, merge, or clean the NSC-020 checkout.
Preserve progress.log, debug.log, progress.jsonl, and the latest Issue event.
"@
    }
}

What caused the new error

The draft contained exact artifact IDs such as:

unity_01_results
unity_01_log
human_validation_01

The proposal validator correctly requires every gate mapping to reference one of those exact IDs. A validation-manifest path, manifest SHA, test filter, display name, or descriptive sentence is not accepted as an artifact ID.

The problem was that our new bounded-history feature compressed the delivery_review_facts result too aggressively. It preserved that there were artifacts and gates, but removed the actual IDs before the next Codex decision. Codex therefore had to guess an artifact identity, and the deterministic validator rejected it.

The TaskDelivery generator itself creates the stable artifact IDs correctly; they simply were not surviving into the proposal-authoring turn.

The run also exposed a smaller routing-order defect on Turn 1. It offered prepare_task_checkout before acquiring the lease. Checkout repair is a side effect, so the lease must always come first.

Both defects are fixed and merged

Carries exact artifact IDs, gate IDs, and candidate surface paths into the proposal turn.

**ChatGPT:**

You were right. There was already committed handling for this recurring Unity-log whitespace—but it did not actually clean the log.

It is now a real pre-merge pipeline step

I merged PR #79. Future authoritative Unity runs now:

Unity writes unity.log
→ remove spaces/tabs at line endings
→ calculate the normalized log SHA and byte size
→ write validation-manifest.json
→ validate the exact normalized artifact
→ package evidence
→ run strict staged whitespace validation
→ commit
→ open PR

The normalizer:

Removes only ASCII spaces and tabs at logical line endings.
Preserves all actual log content.
Preserves CRLF, LF, or CR line endings.
Preserves a UTF-8 BOM.
Preserves whether the file ends with a newline.
Is atomic and safe to run more than once.

The later boundaries now also fail closed:

validation_manifest.py

rejects a dirty Unity log before accepting the manifest;

record_delivery.py

rejects it before copying it into TaskGraph evidence;

and the downstream evidence commit no longer contains the old .log bypass. Every newly generated evidence file must pass whitespace validation. PR #79 is merged as commit 45c48d4afb44c104fd908c3e118f86d7d8e872c0.

Why I did not rewrite NSC-020’s existing log

The existing NSC-020 log was already included in:

human-approved proposal
→ approved review hash
→ delivery record
→ evidence commit

Cleaning that old file now would change its SHA-256 and invalidate the evidence you approved. Therefore, already-approved historical Unity .log artifacts receive a narrow CI compatibility exception. New logs cannot use that exception because they are normalized and verified before their identities are recorded.

Source files, JSON, XML, task contracts, and every non-log path remain under the normal strict whitespace check. The merged workflow now has both the Unity evidence-hygiene test and the narrow historical-log exclusion.

& {
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

    $Repository = "C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\NoSafeCircle"

    Set-Location $Repository

    $env:PYTHONUTF8 = "1"
    $env:NSC_TASK_AGENT_LOG_VERBOSITY = "operator"

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host "MERGE AND COMPLETE THE READY TASK" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host "================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host ""

    Write-Host "=== 1. VERIFY CLEAN CONTROLLER CHECKOUT ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    $Status = @(git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all)

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not inspect the controller checkout."
    }

    if ($Status.Count -gt 0) {
        git status --short --untracked-files=all
        throw "The controller checkout is dirty."
    }

    Write-Host "Controller checkout is clean." -ForegroundColor Green

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 2. UPDATE MAIN ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    git switch main

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not switch to main."
    }

    git pull --ff-only origin main

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not update main."
    }

    $LocalMain = (git rev-parse HEAD).Trim()
    $RemoteMain = (git rev-parse origin/main).Trim()

    Write-Host "Local main:"
    Write-Host "  $LocalMain"

    Write-Host "Remote main:"
    Write-Host "  $RemoteMain"

    if ($LocalMain -ne $RemoteMain) {
        throw "Local main does not equal origin/main."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 3. CONFIRM PR #77 CHECKS ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    gh pr checks 77 `
        --repo cathode26/NoSafeCircle

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "PR #77 does not currently have passing checks."
    }

    Write-Host "PR #77 checks are green." -ForegroundColor Green

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 4. VALIDATE TASKGRAPH ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    python Pipeline/TaskGraph/taskcontrol.py validate

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "TaskGraph validation failed."
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "=== 5. RUN THE GENERIC GAME TASK AGENT ===" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "No task ID is supplied." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host "Issue #64 is queued in merge_closeout." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host "PR #77 has passing checks and is mergeable." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host ""

    $PreviousErrorPreference = $ErrorActionPreference

    try {
        $ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"

        powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass `
            -File .\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1 `
            -ExecutionProvider claude `
            -MaxTurns 120

        $AgentExit = $LASTEXITCODE
    }
    finally {
        $ErrorActionPreference = $PreviousErrorPreference
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "Game Task Agent exit code:"
    Write-Host "  $AgentExit"

    if ($AgentExit -ne 0) {
        throw @"
The Game Task Agent stopped with exit code $AgentExit.

Do not manually alter PR #77, Issue #64, or the task checkout.
Preserve progress.log, debug.log, and progress.jsonl.
"@
    }
}

The expected ending is approximately:

→ verify both checks passed
→ merge with history preserved
→ fetch fresh main
→ verify NSC-020 remains conformant
→ record completion
→ close Issue #64

This recurring Unity whitespace is now handled before evidence identity, before evidence packaging, before commit, and before merge—not merely ignored by one local check.

    -File .\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1 `
    -TaskId NSC-XXX `
    -ExecutionProvider claude `
    -MaxTurns 120

After that first invocation creates the durable Issue, every restart returns to the simple generic command you already know.

What counts as a clean second success

The second canary should complete this exact sequence:

explicit task start
→ one Issue and one task branch
→ Claude implementation and committed handoff
→ your Unity validation
→ generic resume
→ authoritative automated Unity tests
→ delivery-evidence proposal
→ your approval
→ generic resume
→ evidence commit and PR
→ checks finish
→ generic resume
→ merge to main
→ post-merge conformance
→ Issue complete and closed
→ no duplicate Issue

If that happens without a repository patch between stages, then we have meaningful evidence that the pipeline is no longer just capable of one heavily repaired success—it is repeatable.

**ChatGPT:**

Yes. That is the better next test. A second serial task would only test repeatability; two concurrent tasks will test whether the orchestrator actually delivers the parallelism it was built for.

We have a clean starting point: main is currently at 03ec56e..., containing the completed-task duplicate-Issue fix; NSC-020 is closed in the durable complete state; and there are no open agent-ready task Issues.

One critical change: no generic command

We should not open two windows and run the generic command in both.

Your two attached runs both selected NSC-020 because it was the first validated agent-ready Issue.   The selector literally takes the first entry from the ready queue, so simultaneous generic workers could both initially target the same task.

For this canary, every start and every resume must therefore use:

-TaskId <the exact task>
-WorkerId <a stable worker assigned to that task>

The launcher supports both parameters.

How we should run the concurrency test
Choose two genuinely independent tasks. Both must be active, concrete, single_agent, not_delivered, and have conformant dependencies. They cannot depend on one another or share any exclusive_resources.
Stagger only the bootstrap. Start Task A and wait until it has created its Issue, acquired its lease, and prepared its isolated checkout. Then start Task B. Their Claude implementation runs will overlap, but we avoid racing two Docker image builds and two initial Issue claims at the exact same millisecond.
Use separate identities and checkouts. For example:
Task A worker: concurrency-canary-nsc-aaa
Task B worker: concurrency-canary-nsc-bbb

The repository’s orchestration rules require separate standalone task checkouts and treat resources held by agent-working, human-action, or blocked tasks as reserved. The implementation checks exclusive resources before granting a lease.

Run work concurrently, but merge serially. Both tasks can implement, test, pause for human validation, produce evidence, and open PRs concurrently. We should not attempt to merge both PRs at the same instant. Merge A, then explicitly resume B so it observes the new main, performs reintegration, and requests revalidation when necessary.
Freeze unrelated repository changes. There is currently an open draft PR #85 concerning Git-history identity sanitization. Leave it unmerged and perform no history rewrite or force-push while these task workflows are active. Its own description says it is only a non-destructive dry run and does not authorize rewriting main.

Even with disjoint resources, the second task may require another human validation after the first gameplay task merges. The current reintegration policy treats non-automation changes on main conservatively. That would be expected behavior, not automatically a defect.

We need a valid pair—not merely two available tasks

For example, NSC-050 and NSC-007 might both look close to executable based on their dependencies, but they are not a concurrency pair. Both reserve the Door Prototype scene and DoorPrototypeSceneBuilder.cs, so the second lease should be rejected as a resource conflict.

Run this read-only report from the main checkout. It will identify dependency-conformant implementation candidates and then calculate every exact-resource-disjoint pair using the same resource-token model as the current orchestrator:

& {
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

    Set-Location "C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\NoSafeCircle"

    git switch main
    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not switch to main."
    }

    git pull --ff-only origin main
    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not update main."
    }

    $DirtyPaths = @(git status --porcelain)
    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not inspect the main checkout."
    }

    if ($DirtyPaths.Count -ne 0) {
        $DirtyPaths | ForEach-Object { Write-Host $_ }
        throw "The main checkout is dirty. Stop before selecting concurrent tasks."
    }

    python Pipeline/TaskGraph/taskcontrol.py validate
    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "TaskGraph validation failed."
    }

    @'
import json
import subprocess
import sys
from itertools import combinations
from pathlib import Path

root = Path.cwd()

result = subprocess.run(
    [
        sys.executable,
        "Pipeline/TaskGraph/taskcontrol.py",
        "states",
        "--json",
    ],
    cwd=root,
    text=True,
    encoding="utf-8",
    capture_output=True,
)

if result.returncode != 0:
    print(result.stdout, file=sys.stderr)
    print(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)
    raise SystemExit(result.returncode)

try:
    state_rows = json.loads(result.stdout)
except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
    raise SystemExit(f"Could not parse task-state output: {exc}")

states = {
    row["task_id"]: row["state"]
    for row in state_rows
}

candidates = []

for path in sorted((root / "Tasks").glob("NSC-*.yaml")):
    try:
        task = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8-sig"))
    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
        raise SystemExit(f"Could not read {path}: {exc}")

    task_id = task["id"]

    if states.get(task_id) != "not_delivered":
        continue
    if task.get("contract_disposition") != "active":
        continue
    if task.get("kind") != "implementation":
        continue
    if task.get("execution_scope") != "single_agent":
        continue
    if task.get("decomposition_state") != "concrete":
        continue

    dependencies = tuple(task.get("depends_on") or ())
    dependency_states = {
        dependency: states.get(dependency, "missing")
        for dependency in dependencies
    }

    dependencies_conformant = all(
        state == "conformant"
        for state in dependency_states.values()
    )

    candidates.append(
        {
            "id": task_id,
            "title": task["title"],
            "dependencies": dependencies,
            "dependency_states": dependency_states,
            "dependencies_conformant": dependencies_conformant,
            "resources": frozenset(task.get("exclusive_resources") or ()),
        }
    )

print()
print("=== CONCRETE SINGLE-AGENT NOT-DELIVERED TASKS ===")

if not candidates:
    print("(none)")

for candidate in candidates:
    readiness = (
        "DEPENDENCIES CONFORMANT"
        if candidate["dependencies_conformant"]
        else "DEPENDENCIES NOT CONFORMANT"
    )

    print()
    print(f"{candidate['id']} — {candidate['title']}")
    print(f"  status: {readiness}")
    print(
        "  dependency states: "
        + (
            json.dumps(candidate["dependency_states"], sort_keys=True)
            if candidate["dependency_states"]
            else "(none)"
        )
    )
    print("  exclusive resources:")

    for resource in sorted(candidate["resources"]):
        print(f"    - {resource}")

eligible = [
    candidate
    for candidate in candidates
    if candidate["dependencies_conformant"]
]

compatible_pairs = []

for first, second in combinations(eligible, 2):
    overlap = first["resources"] & second["resources"]

    if overlap:
        continue

    if second["id"] in first["dependencies"]:
        continue

    if first["id"] in second["dependencies"]:
        continue

    compatible_pairs.append((first, second))

print()
print("=== EXACT-RESOURCE-DISJOINT ELIGIBLE PAIRS ===")

if not compatible_pairs:
    print("(none found)")
else:
    for first, second in compatible_pairs:
        print(
            f"{first['id']} + {second['id']} — "
            f"{first['title']} / {second['title']}"
        )
'@ | python -

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not build the concurrent-task candidate report."
    }

**You:**

After a task is picked, which isnt there a flag set making it unavailable for the next task agent?

**ChatGPT:**

Yes. You are remembering correctly. I overstated the need to supply two explicit -TaskId values.

The “flag” is the task’s durable GitHub Issue lease state:

agent_ready
    ↓ acquire_agent_lease
agent_working

When the lease is acquired, the orchestrator:

changes the Issue label from nsc-state:agent-ready to nsc-state:agent-working;
records the worker_id;
records a unique lease_id;
refuses another worker’s lease request because the state is no longer agent_ready.

The code explicitly distinguishes agent_working_by_worker from agent_working_by_other, and acquire_agent_lease returns blocked unless the Issue remains agent_ready.

The generic queue also only returns Issues that pass issue_is_agent_ready, so an Issue labeled and recorded as agent_working should disappear from the next agent’s selectable queue.

Your own run demonstrates the transition. After Turn 1 completed acquire_agent_lease, NSC-020 had:

labels: ["nsc-state:agent-working"]
state: "agent_working"
worker_id: "task-review-agent-desktop-6lmnlck-210fe7e27e"
lease_id: "bf2e6cb0..."

That makes NSC-020 unavailable to the next generic task agent. 

The only nuance

“Selected” and “leased” happen at slightly different times.

The agent first reads the queue and selects the Issue. Then, on its first action turn, it acquires the durable lease. Therefore, if we launch two agents at the exact same instant, both could theoretically read the same agent_ready Issue before either completes the lease transition. The second should be denied once it tries to acquire the lease, but it might stop instead of automatically choosing another task.

So the right concurrent test is:

Start the first agent normally, without -TaskId.
Wait only until this appears:
[DONE] Turn 1: Completed: Claim the task so this agent can work safely.
result_status=acquired
Immediately start the second generic agent in another PowerShell window.

At that point the first task is already marked agent_working, while its implementation is still running. The second agent should automatically select another task. That tests the intended orchestration much better than manually assigning both task IDs.

So yes: we should rely on the lease mechanism and run two generic agents, with only a brief stagger to ensure the first lease has been committed. If the second agent still chooses the leased task, that is a real orchestrator defect.

**ChatGPT:**

Nothing is wrong with your PowerShell command. What is wrong is that we have been calling it a generic “get work” command when it is only a generic “resume existing work” command.

Your run behaved exactly as the current code tells it to:

Task: resume first validated agent-ready Issue
...
No validated agent-ready Issue exists.
Pass an explicit -TaskId to begin fresh work;
generic resume never silently invents a new task.

The implementation is explicit about this. When -TaskId is omitted, run_pipeline_agent.py calls select_agent_ready_task(). That function searches only for an already-created, validated Issue in nsc-state:agent-ready; when there are none, it raises the exact error you received. It does not inspect the TaskGraph and choose a new task.

Where the lease fits

You were correct about the lease mechanism, but it begins after a task has an Issue.

For an existing task Issue:

agent_ready
→ agent selects Issue
→ acquire_agent_lease
→ agent_working
→ Issue disappears from the agent-ready queue

That protects a selected task from being taken by another agent. The lease code blocks another worker unless the state is still agent_ready.

But right now the queue contains:

zero agent-ready Issues

There is therefore nothing for the generic command to select or lease. NSC-020 is complete and closed, and no new task Issues have been initialized.

The command presently has two modes
Start fresh work

Requires an explicit task:

powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass `
    -File .\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1 `
    -TaskId NSC-XXX `
    -ExecutionProvider claude `
    -MaxTurns 120

That initializes the Issue, creates the task branch and checkout, and acquires its lease.

Resume existing work

Omits the task ID:

powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass `
    -File .\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\Start-GameTaskAgent.ps1 `
    -ExecutionProvider claude `
    -MaxTurns 120

That selects the first existing agent_ready Issue. It cannot begin a new TaskGraph task.

I gave you incorrect guidance

My previous statement that we could start the first generic worker, wait for its lease, and then start a second generic worker was wrong for a completely empty queue.

That approach would work only after at least two task Issues had already been initialized. Right now neither concurrent task exists in the Issue workflow, so both must first be bootstrapped explicitly.

What is actually missing

We have not yet built the full generic “get me new work” dispatcher you originally wanted.

That command should behave like this:

1. Look for existing agent-ready Issues.
2. Resume the highest-priority existing Issue when one exists.
3. Otherwise inspect not-delivered TaskGraph contracts.
4. Exclude tasks whose dependencies are not conformant.
5. Exclude aggregate, decomposed, cancelled, or non-single-agent tasks.
6. Exclude tasks whose resources conflict with active Issues.
7. Deterministically select the highest-priority eligible task.
8. Atomically create its Issue and acquire its lease.
9. Start implementation.

The repository currently refuses step 3 because autonomous dispatch authority is explicitly disabled. That was a safety boundary while we were proving the pipeline, but it means the command is not yet the seamless general-work command we were aiming to build.

So the honest state is:

We successfully built generic durable resume. We have not yet built generic fresh-work selection.

For the immediate two-task concurrency test, we must choose two compatible task IDs and bootstrap each once with -TaskId. After those Issues exist, their leases and generic-resume behavior can manage the concurrent workflow. The better long-term correction is to implement the safe fresh-task dispatcher so this same simple command can truly mean “get work,” whether work already exists or not.

**You:**

generic-get-work-20260829-191512.zip

* * *

**ChatGPT:**

I reviewed the full Claude package

Claude’s core architecture is right:

normal command
    ↓
resume existing agent-ready work first
    ↓ if none exists
deterministically select authorized fresh work
    ↓
atomically claim task + exclusive resources
    ↓
initialize Issue, lease, branch, and checkout

The key design choices are solid:

The LLM does not choose the task.
Priority comes from a committed, human-reviewed dispatch policy rather than NSC number order.
Fresh selection uses TaskGraph, dependency, Issue, branch, resource, and WIP facts.
Two workers use an atomic remote coordination primitive rather than GitHub labels as a lock.
The existing Issue workflow, human validation, evidence, PR, and closeout pipeline remains intact.
Rollout is staged and initially disabled rather than turning on autonomous dispatch in one large change. 

I would not implement the synthesis verbatim, though. It contains several important mistakes.

Corrections to Claude’s design
1. needs_testing dependencies must remain non-blocking

Claude’s final eligibility rule says every dependency must currently be:

conformant

That directly conflicts with the repository’s established rule. A dependency in needs_testing represents previously delivered work with revalidation debt; it must not block downstream work solely because its latest conformance surface changed. The repository already distinguishes “completed dependency satisfaction” from “currently proven conformant.”

The correct dependency-satisfaction rule for the first dispatcher should be approximately:

satisfied:
    conformant
    needs_testing

not satisfied:
    not_delivered
    needs_replan
    needs_human
    invalid_evidence
    ambiguous_evidence
    aggregate/incomplete
    cancelled
    superseded

needs_testing should still be reported as revalidation debt, but it cannot become a generic skip reason.

2. A machine-stable worker ID is unsafe for two local workers

Claude proposed replacing the per-run UUID with one stable identity per machine so a crashed run can resume. 

That breaks the exact feature we are building. Two PowerShell windows on the same computer would present the same worker ID. The second process could then be mistaken for the first process and receive:

agent_working_by_worker

instead of:

agent_working_by_other

We should retain a unique worker/run identity per process.

Crash recovery must come from explicit claim and lease recovery—not identity impersonation. For the first production version:

claim refs protect the short selection/Issue-initialization transaction;
Issue leases remain uniquely owned by one run;
controlled failures release the lease as they do now;
a doctor command reports a genuinely abandoned hard-crash lease;
stale-lease takeover is a later, explicitly designed transition rather than silently reusing a machine identity.
3. Claim creation needs an explicit nonexistence CAS

Claude correctly chose a multi-ref atomic push for the task claim and all exclusive-resource claims. 

But claim creation should not rely only on the normal non-fast-forward rule. Each created ref should explicitly require that the remote ref does not exist, conceptually:

--force-with-lease=<claim-ref>:

combined with:

--atomic

Git documents that --atomic makes all remote ref updates succeed or fail together, and that an explicit empty expected value for --force-with-lease=<ref>: means the ref must not already exist. Git+1 (https://git-scm.com/docs/git-push/2.40.0?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

The implementation also needs an opt-in live GitHub capability test before production activation. GitHub accepts fully qualified references, but we should prove that this repository and transport accept our chosen custom namespace and atomic multi-ref operation instead of assuming it. GitHub Docs (https://docs.github.com/en/rest/git/refs?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

The test should create and fenced-delete disposable refs only. If GitHub rejects:

refs/nsc/claims/...

the fallback should be a clearly namespaced branch area such as:

refs/heads/nsc-claims/...
4. Public-repository author validation is mandatory

Claude left repository visibility as an open question. It is not open: NoSafeCircle is public.

That means Stage 0 must validate the author of:

the managed Issue itself;
workflow-event comments;
human-result comments;
approval and unblock comments.

Otherwise, an outside commenter could imitate workflow syntax, submit a fake human result, or create an Issue that collides with an NSC task identity.

The initial actor policy should authorize at least:

human/operator:
    cathode26

workflow automation:
    github-actions[bot]

A human PASS must specifically come from the authorized human account—not merely any allowed automation actor.

5. Automatic stale-claim deletion should wait

The synthesis proposes automatic deletion of claim refs after a 60-minute TTL. 

For the first two-worker canary, that is unnecessary risk. A ref can be fenced by its exact SHA, but there is still a timing gap between checking ownership and writing the corresponding GitHub Issue update.

Initial behavior should be:

stale claim found
→ do not dispatch that task
→ report exact ref, owner receipt, age, and task
→ doctor offers an exact-SHA repair command
→ human-authorized cleanup

Automatic garbage collection can come after the claim and crash tests prove the necessary fencing behavior.

Decisions I am adopting

Based on your stated goal of running two tasks concurrently:

max_agent_working:         2
max_open_managed_issues:   4

Version 1 will dispatch only:

kind: implementation
execution_scope: single_agent
decomposition_state: concrete

own state: not_delivered

An explicit -TaskId will skip automatic lane selection, but it will not bypass:

dependency safety;
resource conflicts;
existing Issue/branch/claim ownership;
task shape;
completed-task protection;
the two-worker WIP limit.

If an emergency WIP override is ever needed, it should require a separate explicit flag and a durable override receipt. Merely typing a task ID should not quietly start a third concurrent task.

Priority lanes will not be guessed now. Stage 2 will generate a dry-run dispatch report from the live TaskGraph, and you will approve the initial lane order before enabled becomes true.

**ChatGPT:**

The Stage 0 script did not run

This was my script-generation error, not a pipeline or Claude failure.

The Fable design review completed successfully. The later Stage 0 launcher broke immediately after printing its first header. PowerShell then printed most of the script itself, with variables such as $status, $LASTEXITCODE, and $promptPath disappearing and backticks being interpreted inside the printed text.  The design review itself completed all four reviews and its final synthesis normally. 

Exact cause

I saved the launcher as UTF-8 without a byte-order mark and included a Unicode em dash here:

GENERIC GET-WORK — STAGE 0 SAFETY IMPLEMENTATION

Windows PowerShell 5.1 can interpret a UTF-8-without-BOM file using the Windows ANSI code page. The em dash became mojibake containing a smart closing quotation mark. PowerShell recognizes smart quotation marks as actual string delimiters, so that character prematurely closed the Write-Host string.

The rest of the file was then parsed as part of one large interpolated string. That explains all of these symptoms:

$status became blank
$LASTEXITCODE became blank
backticks disappeared or became escapes
the remaining commands were printed instead of executed

Because the corruption happened before:

git switch main
git pull
git switch -c generic-get-work-stage0-safety
docker compose ...

none of those Stage 0 commands ran.

Therefore:

No Stage 0 branch should have been created.
Claude did not edit the repository.
Nothing was committed or pushed.
generic-get-work-stage0.patch should be empty unless unrelated changes already existed.

Do not rerun the old script.

Corrected package

I rebuilt it with:

An ASCII-only PowerShell launcher.
UTF-8 with BOM for Windows PowerShell 5.1.
The long Claude prompt in a separate text file so PowerShell never parses its backticks or examples.
A prompt-length integrity check.
Verification that Claude did not commit or push.
Verification that Claude actually produced repository changes.
Host-side Python compilation, TaskGraph validation, and git diff --check.

Download the corrected Stage 0 package

Extract, syntax-check, and run it

Use this single PowerShell command:

**ChatGPT:**

Stage 0 is not ready to commit yet

I inspected the complete review archive, including the 24-file binary patch and validation log.

The clean-clone validation did not run a single test. The verifier generated this invalid path:

...\validation-repository\itory\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\tests\actor_authorization_smoke_test.py

The cause is the verifier calculating a relative path with:

$test.FullName.Substring($validationRepo.Length + 1)

Windows exposed $env:TEMP through an 8.3 short path such as VINCEN~1, while FileInfo.FullName expanded it to VincentLiguori. Substring lengths from those two path representations do not match, producing the stray itory\ segment. The verifier source contains that exact substring calculation. 

So the archive contains a valid-looking complete patch, but it contains no clean-clone test evidence.

What is good in the patch

Several changes are directionally correct:

It replaces synthesized empty-resource task objects with a shared committed-task loader.
It makes an open agent_ready workflow reserve its task resources.
It paginates GitHub Issues rather than silently stopping after 1,000.
It binds a human result to the current handoff event and exact tested commit.
It makes the handoff example use placeholders instead of a literal PASS.
It introduces actor authorization for the public repository.
It keeps fresh autonomous dispatch disabled during Stage 0.

Those are worthwhile fixes.

What still needs repair

My manual review found seven blockers.

1. Four-backtick fences can bypass the parser

The new fence stripper remembers only the first three backticks or tildes. A four-backtick outer block containing a three-backtick line can close prematurely, allowing quoted Result: PASS text later in that block to become parseable.

It needs to remember the marker character and the complete opening run length.

2. Bot identities can collapse into human identities

The implementation strips [bot] from every login before authorization. Its own report says GitHub’s two automation representations are normalized by folding the suffix. 

That creates an unsafe consequence:

cathode26[bot]
→ cathode26
→ authorized human

Human authorization must use exact case-insensitive identity. The two GitHub Actions names should be listed as explicit automation aliases:

github-actions
github-actions[bot]

No bot suffix should ever be removed to derive a human identity.

3. An outside commenter can poison the workflow

The current patch makes an unauthorized event-shaped comment invalidate the entire managed Issue. Its tests deliberately expect the Issue to become conflict. 

Because the repository is public, anyone could paste an nsc-workflow-event block and halt a task. That directly conflicts with the Stage 0 requirement that untrusted commenters must not create authority or poison an event chain. 

Unauthorized comments should:

create no event
alter no state
invalidate no authorized history

They may appear in non-authoritative diagnostics, but they must not block work.

Authorized malformed or hash-invalid events should still fail closed.

4. An unauthorized Issue can block resources

The patch can treat an outsider’s fake managed Issue as a coordination conflict and block a legitimate task.

An unauthorized Issue must never:

become workflow authority;
reserve a resource;
prevent a legitimate Issue from being initialized.

An authorized managed Issue with an invalid state or event chain should block until repaired. Those are different cases.

5. Unknown outcomes still exit successfully

The patch changes:

"succeeded"

to:

"unknown_outcome"

for the progress label, but run_pipeline_agent.main() still prints the result and returns:

return 0

So PowerShell still receives success. The Stage 0 requirement explicitly says missing or malformed outcomes must not be reported as successful work. 

This needs an entry-point-level failure, not just a different display string.

6. Paginated Issues can expose API URLs instead of browser URLs

The REST pagination response contains both:

url       = API endpoint
html_url  = browser page

The workflow snapshot still reads url, so some Issue links may become api.github.com/... instead of the normal GitHub Issue page. The backend should normalize html_url into the existing user-facing url field.

7. The new security tests are not wired into CI

Claude added:

actor_authorization_smoke_test.py
committed_task_loader_smoke_test.py
resource_reservation_smoke_test.py

but did not update the workflow that explicitly enumerates TaskReviewAgent smoke tests. The implementation report confirms that the workflow YAML was not modified. 

These tests need named CI steps before Stage 0 can merge.

Repair package

I prepared one bounded repair-and-validation package. It:

Runs Claude Fable 5 against the existing uncommitted Stage 0 branch.
Repairs only the seven issues above.
Keeps Stage 1 and fresh dispatch out of scope.
Verifies Claude did not commit or move HEAD.
Builds a complete patch including untracked files.
Applies it to a clean remote clone.
Runs every TaskReviewAgent smoke test.
Runs the history-migration test, compilation, TaskGraph validation, and whitespace checks.
Uses absolute test paths, eliminating the validation-repository\itory\... verifier bug.
Leaves your real branch uncommitted and unpushed.
