**ChatGPT:**

Right now, we can’t tell from that window alone. The launcher is running Claude in noninteractive -p mode, and that can sit silent until Claude finishes. So after:

Container nosafecircle-claude-run-afd95064041d Created

silence could mean:

Claude is thinking
Claude is waiting on the API
Claude is using tools
Claude is hung

The current launcher gives us no heartbeat. That is an observability gap we should fix.

For the run that is active right now, open a second PowerShell window and paste this:

$Container="nosafecircle-claude-run-afd95064041d"; while ($true) { Write-Host "`n================ $(Get-Date -Format 'HH:mm:ss') ================" -ForegroundColor Cyan; docker ps --filter "name=$Container" --format "Container: {{.Names}} | {{.Status}}"; Write-Host "`n--- PROCESS ---" -ForegroundColor Yellow; docker top $Container; Write-Host "`n--- RESOURCES ---" -ForegroundColor Yellow; docker stats --no-stream --format "CPU={{.CPUPerc}} MEM={{.MemUsage}} NET={{.NetIO}} PIDS={{.PIDs}}" $Container; Write-Host "`n--- MOST RECENT TASKREVIEWAGENT FILE WRITES ---" -ForegroundColor Yellow; Get-ChildItem "C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\NoSafeCircle\Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent" -Recurse -File | Sort-Object LastWriteTime -Descending | Select-Object -First 8 FullName,LastWriteTime; Start-Sleep -Seconds 15 }

What you want to see is:

Container: ... | Up ...

and docker top should show a claude process.

Then watch successive samples. Any of these are evidence it is doing work:

CPU changes
NET I/O increases
TaskReviewAgent file modification times change

If the container disappears, Claude has exited and the first PowerShell window should soon continue with validation.

If it remains Up but for, say, 5–10 minutes you see:

0% CPU repeatedly
no increase in NET I/O
no changed file timestamps

then I would become suspicious that it is stuck. That still isn't absolute proof because it can be waiting on a remote request, but it is enough that we should investigate rather than blindly wait.

You can stop that monitor with Ctrl+C; it does not affect Claude.

More importantly, we should fix this before continuing the pipeline work. The repair launcher should print something like:

[CLAUDE] 00:00 started
[CLAUDE] 00:15 alive | container up | cpu 1.2% | net 4.8MB/1.1MB
[CLAUDE] 00:30 alive | last repository write 8s ago
[CLAUDE] 00:45 alive | no repository write for 23s
...

and ideally show bounded tool/activity events instead of waiting for the final Claude report.

So your instinct is right: we have good observability in the Game Task Agent now, but this one-off Claude repair harness still has the old “black box while the model works” problem. I would add a watchdog/heartbeat to these Claude implementation runners before we use them again.

selected_resources = ...
if not selected_resources:
    return [], []

That means a task declaring no exclusive resources can proceed even when another authorized managed Issue has a corrupted/invalid workflow chain. We explicitly decided that an authorized invalid managed Issue is a coordination failure whose ownership cannot be trusted, so it must block regardless of whether the candidate happens to declare resources. An unauthorized public Issue should still never block.

So I made one final narrow v3 package. It asks Fable only to repair that invariant and add its regression. Yes — and I changed the concurrency design based on what you just said.

Two agents is no longer a limit. We want N agents to be able to request work simultaneously. The safety rule will be:

any number of workers may ask for work;
only one worker can claim a particular task;
tasks sharing an exclusive resource cannot run together;
disjoint tasks can all run concurrently;
a two-worker test is just an early canary, not a production cap;
later we can add configurable operational limits for things like CI load or human-review backlog, but those will be separate from the concurrency mechanism.

That means if we eventually have 15 eligible tasks that touch different resources, 15 agents should be able to work. If 10 agents simultaneously ask for the same task, the atomic Git CAS should make exactly one win.

No. Docker itself will not stop working just because we shorten the Windows path. In fact, I think we should do it. The current path is unnecessarily expensive on Windows, and we have now lost time to the same path-length class of failure more than once.

I checked the Compose setup. The important Docker mounts are relative, such as:

- .:/workspace
- ./Pipeline/ArchitectureReview/outputs:/workspace/Pipeline/ArchitectureReview/outputs:rw

So Docker does not care whether . is:

C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew\NoSafeCircle

or:

C:\NSC\NoSafeCircle

Inside the containers it still becomes /workspace.

I recommend this exact layout
C:\NSC\NoSafeCircle       <- controller/main checkout
C:\NSC\NSC-021            <- task checkout
C:\NSC\NSC-040
C:\NSC\NSC-044
...

That cuts a lot of characters while preserving the existing conceptual layout.

I would keep the leaf directory named NoSafeCircle, rather than changing it to something like C:\NSC\r. There is a Docker reason for that: Compose's default project name is normally derived from the directory name. Your Compose file has named volumes for claude-config and codex-config. Keeping the folder name NoSafeCircle minimizes the chance that Docker suddenly creates a new Compose project with new empty credential volumes.

So:

GOOD:
C:\NSC\NoSafeCircle

I would avoid:
C:\NSC\repo

unless we standardize all Docker calls on docker compose -p nosafecircle ....

There is repository work we should do along with the move. Right now the orchestrator has this hardcoded default:

_WINDOWS_DEFAULT_ROOT = Path(r"C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew")

although it already supports the environment variable:

NSC_TASK_CHECKOUT_ROOT

And our authoritative checkout-path document currently explicitly declares the old root and tells agents to create task checkouts there.

So if we're going to do this, I don't want to half-do it. We should change the canonical architecture from:

C:\UnityProjects\NoSafeCircleAgentCrew

to:

C:\NSC

Then update the default, authoritative docs, tests, and any real scripts that embed the old path.

One more important point

Even after shortening the root, we should keep core.longpaths=true.

The repository genuinely contains absurdly deep historical artifact paths. Shortening the root gives us a large safety margin, but Git should still be configured for long paths. Our authoritative path document already says long-path support is required during cloning.

So our standard becomes both:

short root
+
core.longpaths=true

rather than depending on either one alone.

Safest way to migrate

I would not simply drag the directory around in Explorer. We have orchestration state that can contain exact checkout paths.

I suggest we make this a small controlled migration before continuing Stage 1:
